## Week 0 — Post-Waco recovery (Mon Oct 5 – Sun Oct 11)

**Phase:** Recovery & transition · **Type:** recovery · **Planned:** 2 h 01 min

Recover from Waco (Oct 4). Sleep, eat well, walk. Light movement only from day 4.

### Monday, Oct 5, 2026

**Objective:** Gentle movement after the race.

#### Recovery walk — 20 min
Recovery · Optional · 20 min · Recovery

**Why:** Post-race recovery: muscle damage from a 70.3 run takes days to resolve. Walking helps without delaying repair. Day 1 after Waco.

*Main set*
- 20 min easy walk outdoors — RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat —
*Fueling:* Prioritise carbohydrate and protein at each meal (≈1.6–2.0 g/kg protein/day) and sleep.
*Cues:* Notice any pain that is not normal post-race soreness
*Success:* Done
*Modifications:* fatigue: Rest instead. | weather: Indoors/mall walk. | time: Any amount. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **1 Kings 19:1–8** (Rest) — After his great victory on Mount Carmel, Elijah collapses in exhaustion and despair. God does not lecture him; an angel lets him sleep, then feeds him — twice — because "the journey is too great for you."
>
> The day after a big effort, God's first answer to Elijah was sleep and food. Your body raced 5:20 yesterday. Today's walk is not laziness; it is the same care God showed Elijah.
>
> _What would it look like to receive rest today as a gift instead of earning it?_

### Wednesday, Oct 7, 2026

**Objective:** Rest and recover — absorb the training.

#### Rest day
Rest · Key — mandatory · — · Rest

**Why:** Planned rest prevents the slow slide into fatigue that ruins key sessions. Sleep 7–9 h, eat normally (do not "save calories" on rest days), walk if you like. Day 3 after Waco — full rest.

*Main set*
- No structured training. Optional: 20–30 min easy walk outdoors. — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* — —
*Fueling:* Carbohydrate ≈3–5 g/kg (250–415 g at 83 kg); protein ≈1.6–2.0 g/kg (135–165 g) spread over 4–5 meals. Hydrate normally; pale-yellow urine is a reasonable everyday check.
*Cues:* Bedtime 30 min earlier than usual; Fill in the daily check-in (sleep, fatigue, soreness)
*Success:* You actually rested
*Modifications:* illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Mark 6:30–32** (Rest) — The disciples return from intense ministry, and Jesus invites them to come away to a quiet place and rest a while, because they had not even had time to eat.
>
> Jesus scheduled rest for people doing good work. A full rest day three days after a 70.3 is not falling behind; it is following his pattern.
>
> _Intention: eat three unhurried meals today._

### Thursday, Oct 8, 2026

**Objective:** Active recovery and feel for the water.

#### Easy recovery swim — 1000
Recovery swim · Optional · 21 min · 1000 yd/m · Easy

**Why:** Low-stress swimming aids recovery and keeps the stroke fresh. First swim back — easy, no clock.

*Warm-up*
- 300 easy — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Main set*
- 5 × 100 easy, alternating free / choice / pull — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Long, relaxed strokes
*Success:* Felt better leaving than arriving
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Lamentations 3:22–24** (Faith in uncertainty) — Even in grief, the writer remembers that God's steadfast love never ceases and his mercies are new every morning.
>
> Your first swim back is a fresh start, not a verdict on Waco. New water, new morning, same faithful God.
>
> _What mercy can I notice in this morning's swim?_

### Friday, Oct 9, 2026

**Objective:** Promote blood flow and recovery without adding meaningful training stress.

#### Recovery spin — 30 min
Recovery ride · Optional · 30 min · Z1 (active recovery)

**Why:** Light movement speeds the feeling of recovery and keeps the habit alive. Legs only — Z1.

*Main set*
- 30 min continuous, flat route or trainer — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10 · _If HR drifts above the target or legs feel heavy, shorten it — this ride should leave you fresher._
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat; trainer is ideal. Smart trainer or quiet road loop; power meter if available, HR strap, two bottles, fan indoors.
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required.
*Cues:* Light hands, relaxed shoulders; Cadence 90+ with almost no pressure on the pedals
*Success:* Finished feeling better than you started; Never exceeded RPE 3
*Modifications:* fatigue: Replace with a 20-min walk or full rest. | weather: Trainer. | time: Any duration ≥15 min still helps; skipping is acceptable. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Proverbs 24:16** (Learning from failure) — The righteous may fall seven times, yet they rise again.
>
> You believe you made pacing mistakes at Waco. Falling is not the opposite of righteousness — staying down is. Today's easy spin is the first pedal stroke of rising again.
>
> _Write down one specific pacing lesson from Waco — without condemnation._

### Saturday, Oct 10, 2026

**Objective:** Active recovery and feel for the water.

#### Mobility & tissue care — 20 min
Mobility · Optional · 20 min · Recovery

**Why:** Hip-flexor, thoracic, and ankle mobility support an aero position you can hold and a relaxed stride. Low cost, high return on a rest day.

*Main set*
- 90/90 hip switches — 2 × 8 per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Half-kneeling hip-flexor stretch with reach — 2 × 45 s per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Thoracic open-books — 2 × 8 per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Knee-to-wall ankle mobilisation — 2 × 10 per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Band shoulder dislocates + wall slides — 2 × 10 — RPE 1–2/10
- Calf/soleus stretch (straight and bent knee) — 2 × 45 s per side — RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Home Mat, band, optional foam roller.
*Fueling:* Rest days still need carbohydrate (≈3–5 g/kg) and protein (≈1.6–2.0 g/kg) — recovery happens today.
*Cues:* Slow breathing; no pain
*Success:* Done, and you feel looser
*Modifications:* fatigue: Optional — skip if you need sleep more. | time: 10 min version: first three exercises. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

## Week 1 — Transition — re-entry (Mon Oct 12 – Sun Oct 18)

**Phase:** Recovery & transition · **Type:** recovery · **Planned:** 6 h 19 min

Re-establish routine. Everything easy. Strength re-introduction. First runs.

### Monday, Oct 12, 2026

**Objective:** Rest and recover — absorb the training.

#### Rest day
Rest · Key — mandatory · — · Rest

**Why:** Planned rest prevents the slow slide into fatigue that ruins key sessions. Sleep 7–9 h, eat normally (do not "save calories" on rest days), walk if you like. Start each week rested.

*Main set*
- No structured training. Optional: 20–30 min easy walk outdoors. — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* — —
*Fueling:* Carbohydrate ≈3–5 g/kg (250–415 g at 83 kg); protein ≈1.6–2.0 g/kg (135–165 g) spread over 4–5 meals. Hydrate normally; pale-yellow urine is a reasonable everyday check.
*Cues:* Bedtime 30 min earlier than usual; Fill in the daily check-in (sleep, fatigue, soreness)
*Success:* You actually rested
*Modifications:* illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Exodus 20:8–11** (Rest) — God commands a Sabbath: six days of work, then a day set apart to rest, as God himself rested after creation.
>
> Every Monday in this plan is a rest day by design. The Sabbath is a command, not a suggestion — rest is built into creation itself.
>
> _How will I set this rest day apart from an ordinary day?_

### Tuesday, Oct 13, 2026

**Objective:** Build aerobic capacity and pedaling efficiency at low physiological cost.

#### Aerobic endurance ride — 45 min
Endurance + skills · Supporting — mandatory · 45 min · Z2 (endurance)

**Why:** Most of a 70.3 bike is ridden in the aerobic domain. Low-intensity volume builds the engine (mitochondrial and capillary adaptations) that lets you hold race power with less fatigue.

*Warm-up*
- 10 min progressive — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
*Main set*
- 18 min steady endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
- 6 × 1 min fast cadence (105–115 rpm) / 1 min normal cadence — RPE 3–5/10 · _Builds neuromuscular smoothness — stay seated and quiet through the hips._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat to gently rolling; trainer fine. Smart trainer or quiet road loop; power meter if available, HR strap, two bottles, fan indoors.
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required.
*Cues:* Conversational effort the whole time; Smooth circles; no mashing on small rises
*Success:* Power/HR stayed in Z2 for ≥90% of the main set; HR drift small on a steady effort
*Modifications:* fatigue: Cut to 30–40 min all Z1–Z2, skip drills. | weather: Trainer. | time: Minimum 40 min; keep the drill block, trim steady riding. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength A — lower-body pull + upper pull — Re-introduction
Strength · Supporting — mandatory · 39 min · RIR 4 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021).

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Trap-bar deadlift (or barbell RDL if no trap bar): 2 × 8–10 @ RIR 4, rest 90 s · _Posterior-chain force for climbing and run propulsion. Cue: Brace, push the floor away, neutral spine._
- Rear-foot-elevated split squat (per leg): 2 × 8–10 @ RIR 4, rest 90 s · _Single-leg strength and hip stability for running. Cue: Front shin near vertical, control the descent 2–3 s._
- Pull-up / chin-up (assisted if needed): 2 × 10–12 @ RIR 3–4, rest 60 s · _Upper-back strength for swim catch and aero posture. Cue: Full hang to chin over bar; ribs down._
- Standing calf raise — slow (3 s down): 2 × 10–12 @ RIR 3–4, rest 60 s · _Gastrocnemius strength/Achilles resilience. Cue: Full range; pause at the top._
- Bent-knee (seated) soleus raise: 2 × 10–12 @ RIR 3–4, rest 60 s · _Soleus takes the highest loads in running. Cue: Knee bent ~90°, heavy load, slow._
- Pallof press (per side): 2 × 10–12 @ RIR 3–4, rest 60 s · _Anti-rotation trunk control for run stability and aero bars. Cue: Press out, hold 2 s, resist rotation._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* Light: ~60–65% of your usual working weight. Re-groove patterns after the race; zero soreness is the goal.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).
*Cues:* Quality over grinding: never to failure; Move the weight with intent on the way up; control down; Lift after the endurance session (≥3–6 h later if possible) to protect key endurance quality
*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Zechariah 4:10** (Patience) — God asks who has despised the day of small beginnings, as the rebuilding of the temple starts modestly.
>
> Forty-five minutes of easy riding and light lifts can feel trivial after racing 5+ hours. Small beginnings are how every block is built.
>
> _Do the small session today with full attention._

### Wednesday, Oct 14, 2026

**Objective:** Improve body position, catch, and breathing rhythm — free speed through efficiency.

#### Technique & feel — 2000
Technique · Supporting — mandatory · 41 min · 2000 yd/m · Easy / aerobic with technique focus

**Why:** Swim speed in age-groupers is limited more by technique (drag, catch) than by fitness. Efficient strokes save energy for the bike and run.

*Warm-up*
- 400 easy freestyle, breathing every 3 strokes — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 drill/swim by 25 (catch-up → fingertip drag) — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Long body line; exhale fully underwater._
*Main set*
- 6 × 50 kick on side with fins (25 each side) — 15 s rest — RPE 3–4/10 · _Head in line, bottom arm extended, hips at the surface._
- 6 × 50 "6-kick switch" drill — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Six kicks on side, one stroke, rotate — teaches rotation and balance._
- 5 × 100 swim, focusing on one cue per 100 (high elbow catch → push past hip → exhale) — 15 s rest — CSS +5 to +9 s/100 · RPE 4–5/10
- 4 × 25 fast with perfect form — 30 s rest — CSS -6 to -2 s/100 · RPE 8–9/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Press the chest down, hips up; Reach long, then catch with the forearm vertical; Exhale steadily underwater; quick inhale
*Success:* Stroke count per length stayed the same or dropped across the 100s; Felt smoother by the end
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Proverbs 4:25–27** (Discipline) — Let your eyes look straight ahead; consider the path of your feet; do not swerve to the right or the left.
>
> Technique swimming is attention training: one cue at a time, straight down the lane. Focus is a discipline before it is a skill.
>
> _Which single stroke cue will I own today?_

### Thursday, Oct 15, 2026

**Objective:** Build running durability and aerobic economy at low injury risk.

#### Easy aerobic run — 30 min + strides
Easy run · Supporting — mandatory · 30 min · Z1–Z2

**Why:** Easy running volume strengthens tendons, bones, and aerobic economy — the foundation for running well off the bike. Keeping easy runs truly easy preserves energy for the sessions that matter (Seiler 2010). First structured run since Waco — stop if anything hurts.

*Main set*
- 24 min easy continuous — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10 · _Easy means you could hold a full conversation. Walk breaks on hills are fine if HR rises above target._
- 4 × 20 s strides / 60–90 s walk-jog — RPE 6–7/10 · _Fast and relaxed — about 5K effort, not a sprint. Tall posture, quick light feet._
*Terrain/equipment:* Soft or mixed surfaces; include crushed-gravel trail when possible (race surface). Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required.
*Cues:* Cadence quick and light (no overstriding); Relaxed hands and jaw; Nose-breathing test: if you cannot, slow down
*Success:* HR stayed under the Z2 ceiling (or RPE ≤3); Finished feeling you could do more
*Modifications:* fatigue: Replace with 30 min walk or bike spin; easy runs are the first sessions to cut when tired. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength B — squat + upper push + single-leg — Re-introduction
Strength · Supporting — mandatory · 44 min · RIR 4 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021).

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Back squat or goblet/safety-bar squat: 2 × 8–10 @ RIR 4, rest 90 s · _Quad & glute strength for bike force and descents. Cue: Controlled depth you own, brace._
- Dumbbell bench press or push-up variation: 2 × 8–10 @ RIR 4, rest 90 s · _Upper-body maintenance; shoulder balance for swimming. Cue: Shoulder blades set, 2 s lower._
- Single-leg RDL (per leg): 2 × 10–12 @ RIR 3–4, rest 60 s · _Hamstring/glute strength & balance for gait. Cue: Hips square; reach long._
- One-arm dumbbell row (per side): 2 × 10–12 @ RIR 3–4, rest 60 s · _Pulling strength & posture. Cue: Pull to the hip; no torso twist._
- Step-up to knee drive (per leg): 2 × 10–12 @ RIR 3–4, rest 60 s · _Running-specific single-leg power. Cue: Drive through the whole foot._
- Copenhagen side plank (per side, seconds): 2 × 20–30 s @ RIR 3–4, rest 60 s · _Adductor strength — groin resilience (soccer history). Cue: Start short-lever (knee) and progress._
- Dead bug: 2 × 10–12 @ RIR 3–4, rest 60 s · _Deep trunk control. Cue: Low back stays down._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* Light: ~60–65% of your usual working weight. Re-groove patterns after the race; zero soreness is the goal.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).
*Cues:* Quality over grinding: never to failure; Move the weight with intent on the way up; control down; Lift after the endurance session (≥3–6 h later if possible) to protect key endurance quality
*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Isaiah 40:28–31** (Perseverance) — The everlasting God does not grow weary; he gives power to the faint, and those who wait on him renew their strength — they run and are not weary.
>
> Your first run back. The promise is not that you will never tire, but that strength is renewed by waiting on God — and waiting is what easy running teaches.
>
> _Run today as an act of waiting, not proving._

### Friday, Oct 16, 2026

**Objective:** Improve body position, catch, and breathing rhythm — free speed through efficiency.

#### Technique & feel — 2200
Technique · Supporting — mandatory · 45 min · 2200 yd/m · Easy / aerobic with technique focus

**Why:** Swim speed in age-groupers is limited more by technique (drag, catch) than by fitness. Efficient strokes save energy for the bike and run.

*Warm-up*
- 400 easy freestyle, breathing every 3 strokes — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 drill/swim by 25 (catch-up → fingertip drag) — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Long body line; exhale fully underwater._
*Main set*
- 6 × 50 kick on side with fins (25 each side) — 15 s rest — RPE 3–4/10 · _Head in line, bottom arm extended, hips at the surface._
- 6 × 50 "6-kick switch" drill — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Six kicks on side, one stroke, rotate — teaches rotation and balance._
- 7 × 100 swim, focusing on one cue per 100 (high elbow catch → push past hip → exhale) — 15 s rest — CSS +5 to +9 s/100 · RPE 4–5/10
- 4 × 25 fast with perfect form — 30 s rest — CSS -6 to -2 s/100 · RPE 8–9/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Press the chest down, hips up; Reach long, then catch with the forearm vertical; Exhale steadily underwater; quick inhale
*Success:* Stroke count per length stayed the same or dropped across the 100s; Felt smoother by the end
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Psalm 46:10** (Trust) — Be still and know that I am God.
>
> Stillness in the water — a relaxed head, a quiet kick — makes you faster. Stillness of soul does the same for your life.
>
> _Spend 2 minutes in silence before or after the swim._

### Saturday, Oct 17, 2026

**Objective:** Extend aerobic durability and practice race nutrition.

#### Long aerobic ride — 1:30 h
Long aerobic ride · Supporting — mandatory · 1 h 30 min · Z2 endurance

**Why:** Long rides develop the fatigue resistance that decides the second half of the bike and the whole run. Easy aerobic ride — reconnect with the bike, no targets beyond Z2.

*Warm-up*
- 20 min progressive from easy to endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
*Main set*
- 24 min endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _First fuel at 20 min. Settle in; this is where Waco-style over-pacing starts if you let it._
- 36 min endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _Hold form as fatigue builds._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Rolling route similar to the race (gradual rises, long open roads); stay in aero on safe straights. Trainer if weather or safety requires. Race bike in race position, power meter/HR, race nutrition, saddle bag with tube/CO₂/levers, phone, ID.
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Eat every 20 min, drink every 10–15 min; In aero: chin down, shoulders relaxed, elbows narrow; On rises: shift early, hold power, do not chase speed; On descents: keep light pressure, do not coast if speed is low
*Success:* Normalized power ≤ 1.05 × average power (Variability Index ≤1.05) on steady segments; Hit fueling target (log grams/hour); Blocks within target; final hour not slower than the first
*Modifications:* fatigue: Remove the blocks and ride 70–80% of the duration in Z2. If fatigue is high for 2+ days, ride 60–75 min easy instead. | weather: Rain, ice, lightning, or wind gusts >25 mph: ride indoors at 80–90% duration (indoor riding is harder per minute — continuous pedaling, more heat). | time: Minimum 75% of duration with all blocks intact; trim endurance riding first. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Ecclesiastes 3:1–8** (Patience) — There is a time for everything: a time to plant and a time to harvest, a time to tear down and a time to build.
>
> This is a season of planting. Long, easy rides do not look impressive, but harvest in March depends on what you plant in October.
>
> _What is this season asking of me — in training and beyond it?_

### Sunday, Oct 18, 2026

**Objective:** Build running durability and aerobic economy at low injury risk.

#### Easy aerobic run — 45 min
Easy run · Supporting — mandatory · 45 min · Z1–Z2

**Why:** Easy running volume strengthens tendons, bones, and aerobic economy — the foundation for running well off the bike. Keeping easy runs truly easy preserves energy for the sessions that matter (Seiler 2010). Longest run of the week, all easy.

*Main set*
- 45 min easy continuous — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10 · _Easy means you could hold a full conversation. Walk breaks on hills are fine if HR rises above target._
*Terrain/equipment:* Soft or mixed surfaces; include crushed-gravel trail when possible (race surface). Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required.
*Cues:* Cadence quick and light (no overstriding); Relaxed hands and jaw; Nose-breathing test: if you cannot, slow down
*Success:* HR stayed under the Z2 ceiling (or RPE ≤3); Finished feeling you could do more
*Modifications:* fatigue: Replace with 30 min walk or bike spin; easy runs are the first sessions to cut when tired. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Psalm 118:24** (Gratitude) — This is the day the Lord has made; let us rejoice and be glad in it.
>
> Some long runs are hard; this one is simply a gift. Run it with gladness.
>
> _Name three things you are grateful for on the run._

## Week 2 — Transition — rebuild rhythm (Mon Oct 19 – Sun Oct 25)

**Phase:** Recovery & transition · **Type:** recovery · **Planned:** 7 h 47 min

Light tempo touches; strength re-introduction continues.

### Monday, Oct 19, 2026

**Objective:** Rest and recover — absorb the training.

#### Rest day
Rest · Key — mandatory · — · Rest

**Why:** Planned rest prevents the slow slide into fatigue that ruins key sessions. Sleep 7–9 h, eat normally (do not "save calories" on rest days), walk if you like.

*Main set*
- No structured training. Optional: 20–30 min easy walk outdoors. — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* — —
*Fueling:* Carbohydrate ≈3–5 g/kg (250–415 g at 83 kg); protein ≈1.6–2.0 g/kg (135–165 g) spread over 4–5 meals. Hydrate normally; pale-yellow urine is a reasonable everyday check.
*Cues:* Bedtime 30 min earlier than usual; Fill in the daily check-in (sleep, fatigue, soreness)
*Success:* You actually rested
*Modifications:* illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Matthew 11:28–30** (Rest) — Jesus invites the weary and burdened to come to him for rest; his yoke is easy and his burden light.
>
> Graduate school, work, training: you carry a lot. Rest days are a weekly chance to bring that load to Jesus.
>
> _What burden am I carrying that I need to hand over?_

### Tuesday, Oct 20, 2026

**Objective:** Build aerobic capacity and pedaling efficiency at low physiological cost.

#### Aerobic endurance ride — 60 min
Endurance + skills · Supporting — mandatory · 1 h · Z2 (endurance)

**Why:** Most of a 70.3 bike is ridden in the aerobic domain. Low-intensity volume builds the engine (mitochondrial and capillary adaptations) that lets you hold race power with less fatigue.

*Warm-up*
- 10 min progressive — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
*Main set*
- 33 min steady endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
- 4 × (1 min right leg / 1 min left leg / 1 min both) — trainer only — RPE 3–4/10 · _Eliminate the dead spot at the top of the stroke. Outdoors: replace with 4 × 3 min at 75 rpm in a big gear._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat to gently rolling; trainer fine. Smart trainer or quiet road loop; power meter if available, HR strap, two bottles, fan indoors.
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: water to thirst; 0–30 g/h carbohydrate is fine for easy aerobic work. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Conversational effort the whole time; Smooth circles; no mashing on small rises
*Success:* Power/HR stayed in Z2 for ≥90% of the main set; HR drift small on a steady effort
*Modifications:* fatigue: Cut to 30–40 min all Z1–Z2, skip drills. | weather: Trainer. | time: Minimum 40 min; keep the drill block, trim steady riding. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength A — lower-body pull + upper pull — Re-introduction
Strength · Supporting — mandatory · 39 min · RIR 4 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021).

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Trap-bar deadlift (or barbell RDL if no trap bar): 2 × 8–10 @ RIR 4, rest 90 s · _Posterior-chain force for climbing and run propulsion. Cue: Brace, push the floor away, neutral spine._
- Rear-foot-elevated split squat (per leg): 2 × 8–10 @ RIR 4, rest 90 s · _Single-leg strength and hip stability for running. Cue: Front shin near vertical, control the descent 2–3 s._
- Pull-up / chin-up (assisted if needed): 2 × 10–12 @ RIR 3–4, rest 60 s · _Upper-back strength for swim catch and aero posture. Cue: Full hang to chin over bar; ribs down._
- Standing calf raise — slow (3 s down): 2 × 10–12 @ RIR 3–4, rest 60 s · _Gastrocnemius strength/Achilles resilience. Cue: Full range; pause at the top._
- Bent-knee (seated) soleus raise: 2 × 10–12 @ RIR 3–4, rest 60 s · _Soleus takes the highest loads in running. Cue: Knee bent ~90°, heavy load, slow._
- Pallof press (per side): 2 × 10–12 @ RIR 3–4, rest 60 s · _Anti-rotation trunk control for run stability and aero bars. Cue: Press out, hold 2 s, resist rotation._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* Light: ~60–65% of your usual working weight. Re-groove patterns after the race; zero soreness is the goal.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).

*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Press the chest down, hips up; Reach long, then catch with the forearm vertical; Exhale steadily underwater; quick inhale
*Success:* Stroke count per length stayed the same or dropped across the 100s; Felt smoother by the end
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Psalm 42:5–8** (Hope) — Though his soul is downcast and the waves sweep over him, the psalmist preaches hope to himself: by day the Lord directs his love, by night his song is with me.
>
> The psalmist talks to his own soul. When motivation dips mid-set, you can do the same: "Hope in God."

### Saturday, Oct 24, 2026

**Objective:** Extend aerobic durability and practice race nutrition.

#### Long ride + race-specific blocks — 1:45 h
Long ride + race-specific blocks · Supporting — mandatory · 1 h 45 min · Z2 with race-power / tempo blocks

**Why:** Long rides develop the fatigue resistance that decides the second half of the bike and the whole run. Race-power blocks late in the ride teach you what 75–80% FTP feels like when you are already tired. Short tempo touches remind the legs of steady pressure.

*Warm-up*
- 20 min progressive from easy to endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
*Main set*
- 20 min endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _First fuel at 20 min. Settle in; this is where Waco-style over-pacing starts if you let it._
- 3 × 5 min tempo — 76–85% FTP · HR 88–93% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–92 rpm · RPE 5–6/10 · recovery: 5 min endurance between
- 30 min endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _Hold form as fatigue builds._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Rolling route similar to the race (gradual rises, long open roads); stay in aero on safe straights. Trainer if weather or safety requires. Race bike in race position, power meter/HR, race nutrition, saddle bag with tube/CO₂/levers, phone, ID.
*Fueling:* Before: 1–2 g/kg carbohydrate (80–160 g) 2–3 h prior; familiar, low-fiber foods. During: 45–60 g/h carbohydrate starting at 20 min; 500–750 mL/h fluid adjusted to sweat rate and weather; ~300–600 mg sodium per liter. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Eat every 20 min, drink every 10–15 min; In aero: chin down, shoulders relaxed, elbows narrow; On rises: shift early, hold power, do not chase speed; On descents: keep light pressure, do not coast if speed is low
*Success:* Normalized power ≤ 1.05 × average power (Variability Index ≤1.05) on steady segments; Hit fueling target (log grams/hour); Blocks within target; final hour not slower than the first
*Modifications:* fatigue: Remove the blocks and ride 70–80% of the duration in Z2. If fatigue is high for 2+ days, ride 60–75 min easy instead. | weather: Rain, ice, lightning, or wind gusts >25 mph: ride indoors at 80–90% duration (indoor riding is harder per minute — continuous pedaling, more heat). | time: Minimum 75% of duration with all blocks intact; trim endurance riding first. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Galatians 6:9** (Consistency) — Do not grow weary of doing good; in due season we will reap if we do not give up.
>
> Today's tempo touches are seeds. "In due season" — not this week.
>
> _Where am I tempted to give up because results are slow?_

### Sunday, Oct 25, 2026

**Objective:** Build running durability and aerobic economy at low injury risk.

#### Easy aerobic run — 55 min
Easy run · Supporting — mandatory · 55 min · Z1–Z2

**Why:** Easy running volume strengthens tendons, bones, and aerobic economy — the foundation for running well off the bike. Keeping easy runs truly easy preserves energy for the sessions that matter (Seiler 2010).

*Main set*
- 55 min easy continuous — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10 · _Easy means you could hold a full conversation. Walk breaks on hills are fine if HR rises above target._
*Terrain/equipment:* Soft or mixed surfaces; include crushed-gravel trail when possible (race surface). Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required.
*Cues:* Cadence quick and light (no overstriding); Relaxed hands and jaw; Nose-breathing test: if you cannot, slow down
*Success:* HR stayed under the Z2 ceiling (or RPE ≤3); Finished feeling you could do more
*Modifications:* fatigue: Replace with 30 min walk or bike spin; easy runs are the first sessions to cut when tired. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Psalm 19:1–6** (Gratitude) — The heavens declare God's glory; the sun comes out like a champion rejoicing to run its course.
>
> Even the sun "runs its course" with joy. Look up during today's long run — creation is preaching.
>
> _Notice the sky today and give thanks._

## Week 3 — Base 1 — baseline testing (Mon Oct 26 – Sun Nov 1)

**Phase:** Aerobic base + swim technique · **Type:** test · **Planned:** 7 h 47 min

Three weeks after Waco: establish FTP, CSS, threshold pace, and strength baselines.

### Monday, Oct 26, 2026

**Objective:** Rest and recover — absorb the training.

#### Rest day
Rest · Key — mandatory · — · Rest

**Why:** Planned rest prevents the slow slide into fatigue that ruins key sessions. Sleep 7–9 h, eat normally (do not "save calories" on rest days), walk if you like. Rested for tomorrow's FTP test.

*Main set*
- No structured training. Optional: 20–30 min easy walk outdoors. — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* — —
*Fueling:* Carbohydrate ≈3–5 g/kg (250–415 g at 83 kg); protein ≈1.6–2.0 g/kg (135–165 g) spread over 4–5 meals. Hydrate normally; pale-yellow urine is a reasonable everyday check.
*Cues:* Bedtime 30 min earlier than usual; Fill in the daily check-in (sleep, fatigue, soreness)
*Success:* You actually rested
*Modifications:* illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Psalm 4:8** (Rest) — In peace I will lie down and sleep, for you alone, Lord, make me dwell in safety.
>
> Tomorrow is the FTP test. The best preparation is peaceful sleep — and peace comes from trust, not from control.
>
> _Go to bed 30 minutes early tonight._

### Tuesday, Oct 27, 2026

**Objective:** Establish a validated FTP so every power target in the plan becomes personal.

#### 20-minute FTP test — 65 min
Benchmark test · Key — mandatory · 1 h 05 min · Test

**Why:** Without a measured FTP all bike targets are provisional. FTP anchors training zones, race power, and the bike-split prediction. Retesting every 4–8 weeks shows whether the bike plan is working.

*Warm-up*
- 15 min progressive endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
- 3 × 1 min fast cadence / 1 min easy — RPE 4–5/10
- 5 min at threshold effort (RPE 7) to open up — RPE 7/10
- 10 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Main set*
- 20 min maximal sustainable effort — even pacing — RPE 9/10 · _Start at what you believe is sustainable (RPE ~8) for 5 min, settle, and only push in the final 5 min. Record average power, average HR for the full 20 min, and cadence._
*Cool-down*
- 12 min easy spin, cadence 90+ — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Trainer (ERG off) or a safe, flat, uninterrupted road — use the same setup for every retest. Power meter or smart trainer required for FTP. Without power: record average HR of the 20 min (approximates bike LTHR) and use HR + RPE targets.
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Well rested: no hard training in the prior 48 h; Same time of day, bike, and trainer as future retests; Fan on high
*Success:* Even or slightly negative split (last 5 min ≥ first 5 min); Data entered on the Benchmarks page — FTP ≈ 95% of 20-min average power
*Modifications:* fatigue: If unusually tired, move the test 1–2 days later — a test on tired legs produces wrong zones for 4+ weeks. | weather: Indoors. | time: Do not shorten the warm-up; reschedule instead. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength A — lower-body pull + upper pull — Baseline benchmarks
Strength · Supporting — mandatory · 44 min · RIR 2 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021). Log e1RM benchmarks today.

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Trap-bar deadlift (or barbell RDL if no trap bar): 3 × 5 @ RIR 2, rest 2–3 min · _Posterior-chain force for climbing and run propulsion. Cue: Brace, push the floor away, neutral spine._
- Rear-foot-elevated split squat (per leg): 3 × 5 @ RIR 2, rest 2–3 min · _Single-leg strength and hip stability for running. Cue: Front shin near vertical, control the descent 2–3 s._
- Pull-up / chin-up (assisted if needed): 2 × 8–10 @ RIR 3, rest 60–90 s · _Upper-back strength for swim catch and aero posture. Cue: Full hang to chin over bar; ribs down._
- Standing calf raise — slow (3 s down): 2 × 8–10 @ RIR 3, rest 60–90 s · _Gastrocnemius strength/Achilles resilience. Cue: Full range; pause at the top._
- Bent-knee (seated) soleus raise: 2 × 8–10 @ RIR 3, rest 60–90 s · _Soleus takes the highest loads in running. Cue: Knee bent ~90°, heavy load, slow._
- Pallof press (per side): 2 × 8–10 @ RIR 3, rest 60–90 s · _Anti-rotation trunk control for run stability and aero bars. Cue: Press out, hold 2 s, resist rotation._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* Work up over 3–4 sets to a load you can lift for 5 reps with 2 in reserve. Log load × reps — this estimates 1RM (e1RM) for tracking.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).
*Cues:* Quality over grinding: never to failure; Move the weight with intent on the way up; control down; Lift after the endurance session (≥3–6 h later if possible) to protect key endurance quality
*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Psalm 139:23–24** (Honesty) — Search me, God, and know my heart; test me and know my anxious thoughts.
>
> A test does not create your fitness; it reveals it. Ask God for the same honesty about your heart that the power meter gives about your legs.
>
> _Can I accept today's number without inflating or despising it?_

### Wednesday, Oct 28, 2026

**Objective:** Measure Critical Swim Speed to personalise every swim pace.

#### CSS test (400 + 200 time trials) — 2000
Benchmark test · Key — mandatory · 41 min · 2000 yd/m · Test

**Why:** CSS = 200 / (T400 − T200) per unit of distance. It anchors swim zones and the swim-split prediction. Retesting shows whether technique and fitness are translating into speed.

*Warm-up*
- 400 easy — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 drill/swim — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 build to fast — 20 s rest — RPE 5–8/10
- 100 easy — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Main set*
- 400 time trial — push start, maximal even effort — RPE 9/10 · _Record time. Pace evenly: second 200 within ~3 s of the first._
- 200 very easy + 5–8 min rest (full recovery) — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 200 time trial — maximal effort — RPE 9–10/10 · _Record time. Enter both times on the Benchmarks page (pool unit matters)._
*Cool-down*
- 300 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Same pool & unit every retest; Push starts, no dives
*Success:* Both times recorded; T400 > 2 × T200 is normal; if not, the 200 was under-paced
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Isaiah 43:1–2** (Faith in uncertainty) — God says, "Do not fear, for I have redeemed you; when you pass through the waters, I will be with you."
>

#### Strength B — squat + upper push + single-leg — Baseline benchmarks

- Step-up to knee drive (per leg): 2 × 8–10 @ RIR 3, rest 60–90 s · _Running-specific single-leg power. Cue: Drive through the whole foot._
- Copenhagen side plank (per side, seconds): 2 × 20–30 s @ RIR 3, rest 60–90 s · _Adductor strength — groin resilience (soccer history). Cue: Start short-lever (knee) and progress._
- Dead bug: 2 × 8–10 @ RIR 3, rest 60–90 s · _Deep trunk control. Cue: Low back stays down._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10

- 9 × 100 swim, focusing on one cue per 100 (high elbow catch → push past hip → exhale) — 15 s rest — CSS +5 to +9 s/100 · RPE 4–5/10

*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10

### Sunday, Nov 1, 2026

**Objective:** Establish threshold pace and run LTHR to personalise every run target.

#### 30-minute run threshold test — 60 min
Benchmark test · Key — mandatory · 1 h · Test

**Why:** Friel's 30-minute solo time trial estimates threshold pace (average pace) and LTHR (average HR of the final 20 min). These anchor run zones and the run-split prediction.

*Warm-up*
- 15 min easy — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 20 s strides / 60–90 s walk-jog — RPE 6–7/10 · _Fast and relaxed — about 5K effort, not a sprint. Tall posture, quick light feet._
- 3 min easy — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Main set*
- 30 min solo time trial — as fast as you can evenly sustain — RPE 9/10 · _Press LAP at 10 min. Record total distance, average pace, and average HR for the final 20 min. Most athletes start too fast — the first 5 min should feel controlled._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy jog — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat, uninterrupted route or 400 m track. Same route each retest. No treadmill (pace calibration varies). GPS watch + chest HR strap.
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Rested: no hard session in the prior 48 h; Even pacing — think "negative split"
*Success:* Last 10 min ≥ first 10 min pace; Entered on the Benchmarks page
*Modifications:* fatigue: Reschedule 1–3 days — a tired test produces zones that are too slow for weeks. | weather: Heat >80 °F or strong wind distorts pace — move to early morning or a track; record conditions. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Lamentations 3:40–41** (Honesty) — Let us examine and test our ways and return to the Lord, lifting our hearts and hands to God.
>
> The 30-minute test examines your running honestly. Self-examination is not self-punishment; it is the first step toward returning and improving.
>
> _After the test, write one honest sentence about it._

## Week 4 — Base 1 — week 2 (Mon Nov 2 – Sun Nov 8)

**Phase:** Aerobic base + swim technique · **Type:** build · **Planned:** 9 h 23 min

Sweet-spot bike, hills, CSS swimming, heavy strength.

### Monday, Nov 2, 2026

**Objective:** Rest and recover — absorb the training.

#### Rest day
Rest · Key — mandatory · — · Rest

**Why:** Planned rest prevents the slow slide into fatigue that ruins key sessions. Sleep 7–9 h, eat normally (do not "save calories" on rest days), walk if you like.

*Main set*
- No structured training. Optional: 20–30 min easy walk outdoors. — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* — —
*Fueling:* Carbohydrate ≈3–5 g/kg (250–415 g at 83 kg); protein ≈1.6–2.0 g/kg (135–165 g) spread over 4–5 meals. Hydrate normally; pale-yellow urine is a reasonable everyday check.
*Cues:* Bedtime 30 min earlier than usual; Fill in the daily check-in (sleep, fatigue, soreness)
*Success:* You actually rested
*Modifications:* illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Mobility & tissue care — 20 min
Mobility · Optional · 20 min · Recovery

**Why:** Hip-flexor, thoracic, and ankle mobility support an aero position you can hold and a relaxed stride. Low cost, high return on a rest day.

*Main set*
- 90/90 hip switches — 2 × 8 per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Half-kneeling hip-flexor stretch with reach — 2 × 45 s per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Thoracic open-books — 2 × 8 per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Knee-to-wall ankle mobilisation — 2 × 10 per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Band shoulder dislocates + wall slides — 2 × 10 — RPE 1–2/10
- Calf/soleus stretch (straight and bent knee) — 2 × 45 s per side — RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Home Mat, band, optional foam roller.
*Fueling:* Rest days still need carbohydrate (≈3–5 g/kg) and protein (≈1.6–2.0 g/kg) — recovery happens today.
*Cues:* Slow breathing; no pain
*Success:* Done, and you feel looser
*Modifications:* fatigue: Optional — skip if you need sleep more. | time: 10 min version: first three exercises. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Genesis 2:1–3** (Rest) — God finished his work and rested on the seventh day, blessing it and making it holy.
>
> God rested not because he was tired, but because the work was complete and good. Rest is part of good work, not a break from it.
>
> _Thank God for last week's completed work._

### Tuesday, Nov 3, 2026

**Objective:** Raise sustainable power (muscular endurance) with manageable fatigue.

#### Sweet-spot 3 × 10 min — 69 min
Sweet-spot intervals · Key — mandatory · 1 h 09 min · Sweet spot (88–93% FTP)

**Why:** Sweet-spot work (88–93% FTP) builds the ability to hold steady power for long periods — the core of a 56-mile bike split — while costing less recovery than full threshold work in the base phase.

*Warm-up*
- 10 min progressive spin — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _Start at RPE 2 and finish at the top of endurance._
- 3 × 1 min high-cadence spin-ups (100–110 rpm) / 1 min easy — RPE 3–4/10 · _Smooth pedal stroke; no bouncing in the saddle._
- 3 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Main set*
- 3 × 10 min sweet spot — 88–93% FTP · HR 91–96% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 6–7/10 · recovery: 5 min easy spin · _Seated, steady, aero position for the second half of each rep if your bike allows._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy spin, cadence 90+ — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Trainer preferred for control; or a flat/steady road without stops. Smart trainer or quiet road loop; power meter if available, HR strap, two bottles, fan indoors.
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Steady, not surging: power line should be flat; Breathe low and controlled; you could speak short phrases
*Success:* All reps inside the target range; Last rep power within 3% of the first; RPE ≤7
*Modifications:* fatigue: If sleep <6 h, fatigue ≥7/10 or the first rep is a struggle at the low end of the target: drop one rep per set and cap effort at the bottom of the range. If two reps in a row miss by >5%, stop and ride Z2 — that is data, not failure. | weather: Do it indoors. Outdoor wind makes steady power hard; use a flat, safe loop if you must ride outside. | time: Keep warm-up to 12 min and complete two-thirds of the work; do not rush the recoveries. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength A — lower-body pull + upper pull — Strength development
Strength · Supporting — mandatory · 58 min · RIR 2–3 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021).

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Trap-bar deadlift (or barbell RDL if no trap bar): 4 × 5–6 @ RIR 2–3, rest 2–3 min · _Posterior-chain force for climbing and run propulsion. Cue: Brace, push the floor away, neutral spine._
- Rear-foot-elevated split squat (per leg): 4 × 5–6 @ RIR 2–3, rest 2–3 min · _Single-leg strength and hip stability for running. Cue: Front shin near vertical, control the descent 2–3 s._
- Pull-up / chin-up (assisted if needed): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Upper-back strength for swim catch and aero posture. Cue: Full hang to chin over bar; ribs down._
- Standing calf raise — slow (3 s down): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Gastrocnemius strength/Achilles resilience. Cue: Full range; pause at the top._
- Bent-knee (seated) soleus raise: 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Soleus takes the highest loads in running. Cue: Knee bent ~90°, heavy load, slow._
- Pallof press (per side): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Anti-rotation trunk control for run stability and aero bars. Cue: Press out, hold 2 s, resist rotation._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* Heavy but crisp: increase load 2–5% when all sets reach the top of the rep range at RIR ≥2.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).
*Cues:* Quality over grinding: never to failure; Move the weight with intent on the way up; control down; Lift after the endurance session (≥3–6 h later if possible) to protect key endurance quality
*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Proverbs 21:5** (Discipline) — The plans of the diligent lead to profit, but haste leads to poverty.
>
> Sweet-spot intervals reward steady diligence, not haste. Surging above the range is "haste" — pay attention to the power line.
>
> _Ride every rep inside the range._

### Wednesday, Nov 4, 2026

**Objective:** Raise sustainable swim speed (threshold).

#### CSS set 10 × 100 — 2000
Threshold (CSS) · Key — mandatory · 41 min · 2000 yd/m · CSS / threshold

**Why:** CSS work is the most time-efficient way for age-group triathletes to raise sustainable swim pace. A faster CSS means 70.3 race effort (CSS +3–6 s/100) costs less, so you exit the water fresher.

*Warm-up*
- 400 easy freestyle, breathing every 3 strokes — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 drill/swim by 25 (catch-up → fingertip drag) — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Long body line; exhale fully underwater._
*Main set*
- 10 × 100 @ CSS +2 to +4 s/100 — 15 s rest — CSS +2 to +4 s/100 · RPE 6–8/10 · _Even pace. Use the pace clock; stroke count steady._
- 200 pull with buoy, easy-moderate — CSS +5 to +9 s/100 · RPE 4–5/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Hold the same pace on every rep — the last one should not be the slowest; Rotate to breathe, do not lift the head
*Success:* ≥80% of reps within target; Stroke count rose ≤2 per length late in the set
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Easy aerobic run — 30 min
Easy run · Supporting — mandatory · 30 min · Z1–Z2

**Why:** Easy running volume strengthens tendons, bones, and aerobic economy — the foundation for running well off the bike. Keeping easy runs truly easy preserves energy for the sessions that matter (Seiler 2010).

*Main set*
- 30 min easy continuous — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10 · _Easy means you could hold a full conversation. Walk breaks on hills are fine if HR rises above target._
*Terrain/equipment:* Soft or mixed surfaces; include crushed-gravel trail when possible (race surface). Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required.
*Cues:* Cadence quick and light (no overstriding); Relaxed hands and jaw; Nose-breathing test: if you cannot, slow down
*Success:* HR stayed under the Z2 ceiling (or RPE ≤3); Finished feeling you could do more
*Modifications:* fatigue: Replace with 30 min walk or bike spin; easy runs are the first sessions to cut when tired. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Hebrews 12:11** (Discipline) — No discipline seems pleasant at the time, but later it yields a peaceful harvest of righteousness for those trained by it.
>
> CSS sets are uncomfortable. The writer promises fruit "later" to those who are trained — notice the word.
>
> _What discipline am I resisting because it is uncomfortable now?_

### Thursday, Nov 5, 2026

**Objective:** Build running-specific strength and economy with low impact.

#### Hill repeats 6 × 60 s — 41 min
Hill strength · Key — mandatory · 41 min · Effort-based (RPE 7–8)

**Why:** Uphill running loads the calves, glutes, and hips with less impact than flat speed work and improves economy and power. It also teaches effort-based pacing on rises.

*Warm-up*
- 10 min easy jog — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
- Drills: 2 × 20 m each — A-skips, high knees, butt kicks, carioca — RPE 3–4/10
- 4 × 20 s strides / 60–90 s walk-jog — RPE 6–7/10 · _Fast and relaxed — about 5K effort, not a sprint. Tall posture, quick light feet._
*Main set*
- 6 × 60 s uphill (4–7% grade) at RPE 8 — RPE 7–8/10 · recovery: Walk/jog back down (≈1.5× the rep time) · _Drive the knee, push the ground back, stay tall. Same effort each rep, not the same speed._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy jog + 5 min walk — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Steady 4–7% hill (60–100 m). Treadmill at 5–6% works. Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required. Optional: carbohydrate drink or mouth-rinse if training fasted. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Short, quick steps; Arms drive forward and back; Relaxed descent — practice light, quick steps downhill
*Success:* Consistent effort across reps; No calf or Achilles pain during or next morning
*Modifications:* fatigue: If fatigue ≥7/10, soreness ≥6/10 or sleep <6 h: run easy for 60–70% of the time, no intervals. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength B — squat + upper push + single-leg — Strength development
Strength · Supporting — mandatory · 1 h 05 min · RIR 2–3 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021).

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Back squat or goblet/safety-bar squat: 4 × 5–6 @ RIR 2–3, rest 2–3 min · _Quad & glute strength for bike force and descents. Cue: Controlled depth you own, brace._
- Dumbbell bench press or push-up variation: 4 × 5–6 @ RIR 2–3, rest 2–3 min · _Upper-body maintenance; shoulder balance for swimming. Cue: Shoulder blades set, 2 s lower._
- Single-leg RDL (per leg): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Hamstring/glute strength & balance for gait. Cue: Hips square; reach long._
- One-arm dumbbell row (per side): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Pulling strength & posture. Cue: Pull to the hip; no torso twist._
- Step-up to knee drive (per leg): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Running-specific single-leg power. Cue: Drive through the whole foot._
- Copenhagen side plank (per side, seconds): 3 × 20–30 s @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Adductor strength — groin resilience (soccer history). Cue: Start short-lever (knee) and progress._
- Dead bug: 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Deep trunk control. Cue: Low back stays down._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* Heavy but crisp: increase load 2–5% when all sets reach the top of the rep range at RIR ≥2.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).
*Cues:* Quality over grinding: never to failure; Move the weight with intent on the way up; control down; Lift after the endurance session (≥3–6 h later if possible) to protect key endurance quality
*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Habakkuk 3:17–19** (Trust) — Even if crops fail and flocks are gone, the prophet rejoices in God, who makes his feet like a deer's and enables him to tread on the heights.
>
> Hill repeats: feet like a deer on high places. Habakkuk's joy did not depend on circumstances; neither must yours.
>
