### Tuesday, Dec 1, 2026

**Objective:** Lift FTP so race power (75–80% FTP) becomes a smaller fraction of your ceiling.

#### Threshold 4 × 8 min — 73 min
Threshold intervals · Key — mandatory · 1 h 13 min · Z4 threshold (95–100% FTP)

**Why:** Raising FTP is the most direct way to ride faster at the same relative effort. If FTP rises 5%, the same 77% race intensity is ~5% more watts — roughly 3–5 minutes over 56 miles — without costing your run.

*Warm-up*
- 10 min progressive spin — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _Start at RPE 2 and finish at the top of endurance._
- 3 × 1 min high-cadence spin-ups (100–110 rpm) / 1 min easy — RPE 3–4/10 · _Smooth pedal stroke; no bouncing in the saddle._
- 3 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Main set*
- 4 × 8 min at threshold — 95–100% FTP · HR 95–100% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 7–8/10 · recovery: 4 min easy spin · _Settle into the range within 60 s; do not start above it._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy spin, cadence 90+ — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Trainer preferred for control; or a flat/steady road without stops. Smart trainer or quiet road loop; power meter if available, HR strap, two bottles, fan indoors.
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Even pacing across reps; Relax the upper body; tension wastes energy
*Success:* ≥90% of interval time within range; Final rep completed without dropping >5%
*Modifications:* fatigue: If sleep <6 h, fatigue ≥7/10 or the first rep is a struggle at the low end of the target: drop one rep per set and cap effort at the bottom of the range. If two reps in a row miss by >5%, stop and ride Z2 — that is data, not failure. | weather: Do it indoors. Outdoor wind makes steady power hard; use a flat, safe loop if you must ride outside. | time: Keep warm-up to 12 min and complete two-thirds of the work; do not rush the recoveries. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength A — lower-body pull + upper pull — Strength development
Strength · Supporting — mandatory · 58 min · RIR 2–3 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021).

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Trap-bar deadlift (or barbell RDL if no trap bar): 4 × 5–6 @ RIR 2–3, rest 2–3 min · _Posterior-chain force for climbing and run propulsion. Cue: Brace, push the floor away, neutral spine._
- Rear-foot-elevated split squat (per leg): 4 × 5–6 @ RIR 2–3, rest 2–3 min · _Single-leg strength and hip stability for running. Cue: Front shin near vertical, control the descent 2–3 s._
- Pull-up / chin-up (assisted if needed): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Upper-back strength for swim catch and aero posture. Cue: Full hang to chin over bar; ribs down._
- Standing calf raise — slow (3 s down): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Gastrocnemius strength/Achilles resilience. Cue: Full range; pause at the top._
- Bent-knee (seated) soleus raise: 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Soleus takes the highest loads in running. Cue: Knee bent ~90°, heavy load, slow._
- Pallof press (per side): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Anti-rotation trunk control for run stability and aero bars. Cue: Press out, hold 2 s, resist rotation._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* Heavy but crisp: increase load 2–5% when all sets reach the top of the rep range at RIR ≥2.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).
*Cues:* Quality over grinding: never to failure; Move the weight with intent on the way up; control down; Lift after the endurance session (≥3–6 h later if possible) to protect key endurance quality
*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **James 1:12** (Perseverance) — Blessed is the one who perseveres under trial, because after standing the test they will receive the crown of life.
>
> The first threshold session of the plan. Persevering through rep 4 is a small picture of a much larger faithfulness.
>
> _Stay steady through the final rep._

### Wednesday, Dec 2, 2026

**Objective:** Raise sustainable swim speed (threshold).

#### CSS set 4 × 300 — 2200
Threshold (CSS) · Key — mandatory · 45 min · 2200 yd/m · CSS / threshold

**Why:** CSS work is the most time-efficient way for age-group triathletes to raise sustainable swim pace. A faster CSS means 70.3 race effort (CSS +3–6 s/100) costs less, so you exit the water fresher.

*Warm-up*
- 400 easy freestyle, breathing every 3 strokes — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 drill/swim by 25 (catch-up → fingertip drag) — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Long body line; exhale fully underwater._
*Main set*
- 4 × 300 @ CSS +2 to +4 s/100 — 30 s rest — CSS +2 to +4 s/100 · RPE 6–8/10 · _Even pace. Use the pace clock; stroke count steady._
- 200 pull with buoy, easy-moderate — CSS +5 to +9 s/100 · RPE 4–5/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Hold the same pace on every rep — the last one should not be the slowest; Rotate to breathe, do not lift the head
*Success:* ≥80% of reps within target; Stroke count rose ≤2 per length late in the set
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Easy aerobic run — 35 min
Easy run · Supporting — mandatory · 35 min · Z1–Z2

**Why:** Easy running volume strengthens tendons, bones, and aerobic economy — the foundation for running well off the bike. Keeping easy runs truly easy preserves energy for the sessions that matter (Seiler 2010).

*Main set*
- 35 min easy continuous — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10 · _Easy means you could hold a full conversation. Walk breaks on hills are fine if HR rises above target._
*Terrain/equipment:* Soft or mixed surfaces; include crushed-gravel trail when possible (race surface). Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required.
*Cues:* Cadence quick and light (no overstriding); Relaxed hands and jaw; Nose-breathing test: if you cannot, slow down
*Success:* HR stayed under the Z2 ceiling (or RPE ≤3); Finished feeling you could do more
*Modifications:* fatigue: Replace with 30 min walk or bike spin; easy runs are the first sessions to cut when tired. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Psalm 107:23–30** (Trust) — Sailors caught in a storm cry out to the Lord; he stills the storm to a whisper and guides them to their desired haven.
>
> Longer CSS reps can feel like a storm. Settle your breathing, and remember who brings us to the haven.
>
> _Breathe calmly on every wall turn._

### Thursday, Dec 3, 2026

**Objective:** Raise lactate threshold / critical speed — the ceiling under your 70.3 run pace.

#### Threshold cruise intervals 4 × 6 min — 56 min
Threshold intervals · Key — mandatory · 56 min · Z4 threshold

**Why:** Threshold work raises the speed you can sustain before fatigue accelerates (critical speed; Jones & Vanhatalo 2017). Your off-the-bike half-marathon pace is a fraction of it, so a higher ceiling makes race pace easier.

*Warm-up*
- 10 min easy jog — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
- Drills: 2 × 20 m each — A-skips, high knees, butt kicks, carioca — RPE 3–4/10
- 4 × 20 s strides / 60–90 s walk-jog — RPE 6–7/10 · _Fast and relaxed — about 5K effort, not a sprint. Tall posture, quick light feet._
*Main set*
- 4 × 6 min at threshold — 1% faster to 3% slower than threshold pace · HR 95–99% run LTHR · RPE 7–8/10 · recovery: 90 s easy jog · _"Comfortably hard": controlled, rhythmic breathing; the last rep should be the best one._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy jog + 5 min walk — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat loop, track, or crushed-gravel trail; treadmill at 1% acceptable. Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required. Optional: carbohydrate drink or mouth-rinse if training fasted. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Even splits — the first rep is where pacing discipline is built; Relax shoulders on every lap
*Success:* All reps within the pace/HR range; Last rep ≤3 s/mi slower than the first
*Modifications:* fatigue: If fatigue ≥7/10, soreness ≥6/10 or sleep <6 h: run easy for 60–70% of the time, no intervals. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength B — squat + upper push + single-leg — Strength development
Strength · Supporting — mandatory · 1 h 05 min · RIR 2–3 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021).

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Back squat or goblet/safety-bar squat: 4 × 5–6 @ RIR 2–3, rest 2–3 min · _Quad & glute strength for bike force and descents. Cue: Controlled depth you own, brace._
- Dumbbell bench press or push-up variation: 4 × 5–6 @ RIR 2–3, rest 2–3 min · _Upper-body maintenance; shoulder balance for swimming. Cue: Shoulder blades set, 2 s lower._
- Single-leg RDL (per leg): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Hamstring/glute strength & balance for gait. Cue: Hips square; reach long._
- One-arm dumbbell row (per side): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Pulling strength & posture. Cue: Pull to the hip; no torso twist._
- Step-up to knee drive (per leg): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Running-specific single-leg power. Cue: Drive through the whole foot._
- Copenhagen side plank (per side, seconds): 3 × 20–30 s @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Adductor strength — groin resilience (soccer history). Cue: Start short-lever (knee) and progress._
- Dead bug: 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Deep trunk control. Cue: Low back stays down._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* Heavy but crisp: increase load 2–5% when all sets reach the top of the rep range at RIR ≥2.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).
*Cues:* Quality over grinding: never to failure; Move the weight with intent on the way up; control down; Lift after the endurance session (≥3–6 h later if possible) to protect key endurance quality
*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

### Friday, Dec 4, 2026

**Objective:** Improve body position, catch, and breathing rhythm — free speed through efficiency.

#### Technique & feel — 2600
Technique · Supporting — mandatory · 53 min · 2600 yd/m · Easy / aerobic with technique focus

**Why:** Swim speed in age-groupers is limited more by technique (drag, catch) than by fitness. Efficient strokes save energy for the bike and run.

*Warm-up*
- 400 easy freestyle, breathing every 3 strokes — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 drill/swim by 25 (catch-up → fingertip drag) — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Long body line; exhale fully underwater._
*Main set*
- 6 × 50 kick on side with fins (25 each side) — 15 s rest — RPE 3–4/10 · _Head in line, bottom arm extended, hips at the surface._
- 6 × 50 "6-kick switch" drill — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Six kicks on side, one stroke, rotate — teaches rotation and balance._
- 11 × 100 swim, focusing on one cue per 100 (high elbow catch → push past hip → exhale) — 15 s rest — CSS +5 to +9 s/100 · RPE 4–5/10
- 4 × 25 fast with perfect form — 30 s rest — CSS -6 to -2 s/100 · RPE 8–9/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Press the chest down, hips up; Reach long, then catch with the forearm vertical; Exhale steadily underwater; quick inhale
*Success:* Stroke count per length stayed the same or dropped across the 100s; Felt smoother by the end
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Proverbs 1:5** (Teachability) — Let the wise listen and add to their learning, and let the discerning get guidance.
>
> Technique days are learning days. Even a former college athlete is still a student.
>
> _What did I learn today that I did not know last week?_

### Saturday, Dec 5, 2026

**Objective:** Extend aerobic durability and practice race nutrition.

#### Long ride + race-specific blocks — 2:45 h
Long ride + race-specific blocks · Key — mandatory · 2 h 45 min · Z2 with race-power / tempo blocks

**Why:** Climbing-specific blocks build force on gradual rises without surging. Long rides develop the fatigue resistance that decides the second half of the bike and the whole run. Race-power blocks late in the ride teach you what 75–80% FTP feels like when you are already tired.

*Warm-up*
- 20 min progressive from easy to endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
*Main set*
- 36 min endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _First fuel at 20 min. Settle in; this is where Waco-style over-pacing starts if you let it._
- 5 × 6 min seated climbing (low cadence 65–75 rpm) — 85–90% FTP · Cadence 65–75 rpm · RPE 6–7/10 · recovery: 4 min endurance between · _Seated, quiet upper body; a force builder, not a sprint._
- 53 min endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _Final 10 min: shift to an easier gear, spin 90+ rpm, finish the bottle — prepare legs for the brick._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Rolling route with 1–4 min rises (north of Little Elm toward Denton County is ideal), or trainer with grade simulation. Race bike in race position, power meter/HR, race nutrition, saddle bag with tube/CO₂/levers, phone, ID.
*Fueling:* Before: 1–2 g/kg carbohydrate (80–160 g) 2–4 h prior — rehearse your race breakfast. During: 60–90 g/h carbohydrate from glucose + fructose sources (build toward 80–90 g/h by the race-specific phase); 500–800 mL/h fluid adjusted to sweat rate; ~300–600 mg sodium per liter (more if you are a heavy/salty sweater). Log grams per hour and any GI symptoms — this is gut training. Keep eating in the final 20 min of the ride — fuel for the run starts on the bike. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Eat every 20 min, drink every 10–15 min; In aero: chin down, shoulders relaxed, elbows narrow; On rises: shift early, hold power, do not chase speed; On descents: keep light pressure, do not coast if speed is low
*Success:* Normalized power ≤ 1.05 × average power (Variability Index ≤1.05) on steady segments; Hit fueling target (log grams/hour); Blocks within target; final hour not slower than the first
*Modifications:* fatigue: Remove the blocks and ride 70–80% of the duration in Z2. If fatigue is high for 2+ days, ride 60–75 min easy instead. | weather: Rain, ice, lightning, or wind gusts >25 mph: ride indoors at 80–90% duration (indoor riding is harder per minute — continuous pedaling, more heat). | time: Minimum 75% of duration with all blocks intact; trim endurance riding first. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Brick run — 15 min
Brick run · Supporting — mandatory · 15 min · Z2

**Why:** Running economy is impaired immediately after cycling (Millet & Vleck 2000). Regular brick runs train the cycle-to-run transition and teach you to judge race pace when your legs lie to you.

*Warm-up*
- T2 practice: rack bike, helmet off, shoes on, go — target <2 min — RPE 3/10
*Main set*
- 15 min steady aerobic off the bike — 13% slower to 22% slower than threshold pace · HR 85–89% run LTHR · RPE 3–4/10 · _Focus on cadence (≈170–180 spm) in the first 5 min._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min walk — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat loop starting where you finish the ride. Race shoes with elastic laces, race belt, visor, gel.
*Fueling:* Take a gel or 20–25 g carbohydrate in the first 10 min if the run is ≥30 min (practice race-day timing). Drink at a "simulated aid station" every ~1 mile/10 min.
*Cues:* Short quick steps for the first 5 min; Pace by HR/RPE first, then the watch; Note how race pace feels — this calibrates race day
*Success:* Hit pace without exceeding the HR ceiling; Transition <2 min; Log RPE and how legs felt at 5 min vs. 15 min
*Modifications:* fatigue: Run 10 min easy only, or walk 10 min. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: 10 min minimum — the transition itself is the stimulus. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Psalm 125:1–2** (Steadfastness) — Those who trust in the Lord are like Mount Zion, which cannot be shaken; as mountains surround Jerusalem, the Lord surrounds his people.
>
> Climbing day. Seated, steady, unshaken — like a mountain. The Lord surrounds you as surely as hills surround a city.
>
> _Stay seated and steady on every climb._

### Sunday, Dec 6, 2026

**Objective:** Extend running durability so pace holds in the final 5K of the race.

#### Long run — 80 min
Long run · Key — mandatory · 1 h 20 min · Z2 aerobic

**Why:** The 70.3 run is decided by durability: holding form and pace after 3+ hours of racing. Long runs build musculoskeletal resilience and fat oxidation; race-pace segments late in the run rehearse running well while fatigued.

*Warm-up*
- 5 min walk → easy jog — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Main set*
- 75 min easy aerobic — 13% slower to 22% slower than threshold pace · HR 85–89% run LTHR · RPE 3–4/10 · _First 10 min easier than you think — practice the patience you need on race day._
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat-to-gently-rolling, ideally crushed gravel/trail (race surface). Loop past your car or a water fountain for fluids. Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals). Handheld/belt with fluids and gels for runs >75 min.
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Run the hills by effort, not pace; Gel practice at the times you will take them in the race; Check form every 10 min: tall, relaxed, quick
*Success:* Pace in the final 20 min ≥ the first 20 min at similar effort; HR drift <5% in the easy portion (where pace is steady); No pain that alters gait
*Modifications:* fatigue: Run 60–70% of the duration, all easy, no race-pace blocks. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep at least 75% of the duration; race-pace blocks stay. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **1 Corinthians 15:58** (Faithfulness) — Stand firm; let nothing move you; always give yourself fully to the work of the Lord, because your labor is not in vain.
>
> Your labor — in study, work, and training — is not in vain when it is offered to God.
>
> _Offer today's long run to God._

## Week 9 — Base 2 — week 2 (Mon Dec 7 – Sun Dec 13)

**Phase:** Bike power & climbing / run durability · **Type:** build · **Planned:** 11 h 18 min

Longer threshold reps, hill strength running.

### Monday, Dec 7, 2026

**Objective:** Rest and recover — absorb the training.

#### Rest day
Rest · Key — mandatory · — · Rest

**Why:** Planned rest prevents the slow slide into fatigue that ruins key sessions. Sleep 7–9 h, eat normally (do not "save calories" on rest days), walk if you like.

*Main set*
- No structured training. Optional: 20–30 min easy walk outdoors. — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* — —
*Fueling:* Carbohydrate ≈3–5 g/kg (250–415 g at 83 kg); protein ≈1.6–2.0 g/kg (135–165 g) spread over 4–5 meals. Hydrate normally; pale-yellow urine is a reasonable everyday check.
*Cues:* Bedtime 30 min earlier than usual; Fill in the daily check-in (sleep, fatigue, soreness)
*Success:* You actually rested
*Modifications:* illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Mobility & tissue care — 20 min
Mobility · Optional · 20 min · Recovery

**Why:** Hip-flexor, thoracic, and ankle mobility support an aero position you can hold and a relaxed stride. Low cost, high return on a rest day.

*Main set*
- 90/90 hip switches — 2 × 8 per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Half-kneeling hip-flexor stretch with reach — 2 × 45 s per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Thoracic open-books — 2 × 8 per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Knee-to-wall ankle mobilisation — 2 × 10 per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Band shoulder dislocates + wall slides — 2 × 10 — RPE 1–2/10
- Calf/soleus stretch (straight and bent knee) — 2 × 45 s per side — RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Home Mat, band, optional foam roller.
*Fueling:* Rest days still need carbohydrate (≈3–5 g/kg) and protein (≈1.6–2.0 g/kg) — recovery happens today.
*Cues:* Slow breathing; no pain
*Success:* Done, and you feel looser
*Modifications:* fatigue: Optional — skip if you need sleep more. | time: 10 min version: first three exercises. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> _Write down one worry and pray over it._

### Tuesday, Dec 8, 2026

**Objective:** Lift FTP so race power (75–80% FTP) becomes a smaller fraction of your ceiling.

#### Threshold 3 × 12 min — 75 min
Threshold intervals · Key — mandatory · 1 h 15 min · Z4 threshold (95–100% FTP)

**Why:** Raising FTP is the most direct way to ride faster at the same relative effort. If FTP rises 5%, the same 77% race intensity is ~5% more watts — roughly 3–5 minutes over 56 miles — without costing your run.

*Warm-up*
- 10 min progressive spin — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _Start at RPE 2 and finish at the top of endurance._
- 3 × 1 min high-cadence spin-ups (100–110 rpm) / 1 min easy — RPE 3–4/10 · _Smooth pedal stroke; no bouncing in the saddle._
- 3 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Main set*
- 3 × 12 min at threshold — 95–100% FTP · HR 95–100% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 7–8/10 · recovery: 5 min easy spin · _Settle into the range within 60 s; do not start above it._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy spin, cadence 90+ — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Trainer preferred for control; or a flat/steady road without stops. Smart trainer or quiet road loop; power meter if available, HR strap, two bottles, fan indoors.
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Even pacing across reps; Relax the upper body; tension wastes energy
*Success:* ≥90% of interval time within range; Final rep completed without dropping >5%
*Modifications:* fatigue: If sleep <6 h, fatigue ≥7/10 or the first rep is a struggle at the low end of the target: drop one rep per set and cap effort at the bottom of the range. If two reps in a row miss by >5%, stop and ride Z2 — that is data, not failure. | weather: Do it indoors. Outdoor wind makes steady power hard; use a flat, safe loop if you must ride outside. | time: Keep warm-up to 12 min and complete two-thirds of the work; do not rush the recoveries. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength A — lower-body pull + upper pull — Strength development
Strength · Supporting — mandatory · 58 min · RIR 2–3 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021).

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Trap-bar deadlift (or barbell RDL if no trap bar): 4 × 5–6 @ RIR 2–3, rest 2–3 min · _Posterior-chain force for climbing and run propulsion. Cue: Brace, push the floor away, neutral spine._
- Rear-foot-elevated split squat (per leg): 4 × 5–6 @ RIR 2–3, rest 2–3 min · _Single-leg strength and hip stability for running. Cue: Front shin near vertical, control the descent 2–3 s._
- Pull-up / chin-up (assisted if needed): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Upper-back strength for swim catch and aero posture. Cue: Full hang to chin over bar; ribs down._
- Standing calf raise — slow (3 s down): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Gastrocnemius strength/Achilles resilience. Cue: Full range; pause at the top._
- Bent-knee (seated) soleus raise: 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Soleus takes the highest loads in running. Cue: Knee bent ~90°, heavy load, slow._
- Pallof press (per side): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Anti-rotation trunk control for run stability and aero bars. Cue: Press out, hold 2 s, resist rotation._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* Heavy but crisp: increase load 2–5% when all sets reach the top of the rep range at RIR ≥2.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).
*Cues:* Quality over grinding: never to failure; Move the weight with intent on the way up; control down; Lift after the endurance session (≥3–6 h later if possible) to protect key endurance quality
*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

>
> Hard training is easier with others. Who sharpens you — and whom do you sharpen?
>
> _Invite someone to join a session this month._

### Wednesday, Dec 9, 2026

**Objective:** Raise sustainable swim speed (threshold).

#### CSS set 3 × 400 — 2400
Threshold (CSS) · Key — mandatory · 49 min · 2400 yd/m · CSS / threshold

**Why:** CSS work is the most time-efficient way for age-group triathletes to raise sustainable swim pace. A faster CSS means 70.3 race effort (CSS +3–6 s/100) costs less, so you exit the water fresher.

*Warm-up*
- 400 easy freestyle, breathing every 3 strokes — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 drill/swim by 25 (catch-up → fingertip drag) — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Long body line; exhale fully underwater._
*Main set*
- 3 × 400 @ CSS +3 to +5 s/100 — 30 s rest — CSS +3 to +5 s/100 · RPE 6–8/10 · _Even pace. Use the pace clock; stroke count steady._
- 200 pull with buoy, easy-moderate — CSS +5 to +9 s/100 · RPE 4–5/10
- 4 × 50 fast — 30 s rest — CSS -6 to -2 s/100 · RPE 8–9/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Hold the same pace on every rep — the last one should not be the slowest; Rotate to breathe, do not lift the head
*Success:* ≥80% of reps within target; Stroke count rose ≤2 per length late in the set
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Easy aerobic run — 40 min
Easy run · Supporting — mandatory · 40 min · Z1–Z2

**Why:** Easy running volume strengthens tendons, bones, and aerobic economy — the foundation for running well off the bike. Keeping easy runs truly easy preserves energy for the sessions that matter (Seiler 2010).

*Main set*
- 40 min easy continuous — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10 · _Easy means you could hold a full conversation. Walk breaks on hills are fine if HR rises above target._
*Terrain/equipment:* Soft or mixed surfaces; include crushed-gravel trail when possible (race surface). Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required.
*Cues:* Cadence quick and light (no overstriding); Relaxed hands and jaw; Nose-breathing test: if you cannot, slow down
*Success:* HR stayed under the Z2 ceiling (or RPE ≤3); Finished feeling you could do more
*Modifications:* fatigue: Replace with 30 min walk or bike spin; easy runs are the first sessions to cut when tired. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Mark 4:35–41** (Faith in uncertainty) — Jesus sleeps through a storm; the frightened disciples wake him, and he calms the wind and waves, asking why they are afraid.
>
> Open-water fear is real. The question Jesus asks is not "why is there a storm?" but "why are you afraid?"
>
> _What fear do I bring to the water?_

### Thursday, Dec 10, 2026

**Objective:** Build running-specific strength and economy with low impact.

#### Hill repeats 8 × 90 s — 55 min
Hill strength · Key — mandatory · 55 min · Effort-based (RPE 7–8)

**Why:** Uphill running loads the calves, glutes, and hips with less impact than flat speed work and improves economy and power. It also teaches effort-based pacing on rises.

*Warm-up*
- 10 min easy jog — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
- Drills: 2 × 20 m each — A-skips, high knees, butt kicks, carioca — RPE 3–4/10
- 4 × 20 s strides / 60–90 s walk-jog — RPE 6–7/10 · _Fast and relaxed — about 5K effort, not a sprint. Tall posture, quick light feet._
*Main set*
- 8 × 90 s uphill (4–7% grade) at RPE 8 — RPE 7–8/10 · recovery: Walk/jog back down (≈1.5× the rep time) · _Drive the knee, push the ground back, stay tall. Same effort each rep, not the same speed._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy jog + 5 min walk — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Steady 4–7% hill (60–100 m). Treadmill at 5–6% works. Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required. Optional: carbohydrate drink or mouth-rinse if training fasted. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Short, quick steps; Arms drive forward and back; Relaxed descent — practice light, quick steps downhill
*Success:* Consistent effort across reps; No calf or Achilles pain during or next morning
*Modifications:* fatigue: If fatigue ≥7/10, soreness ≥6/10 or sleep <6 h: run easy for 60–70% of the time, no intervals. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength B — squat + upper push + single-leg — Strength development
Strength · Supporting — mandatory · 1 h 05 min · RIR 2–3 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021).

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Back squat or goblet/safety-bar squat: 4 × 5–6 @ RIR 2–3, rest 2–3 min · _Quad & glute strength for bike force and descents. Cue: Controlled depth you own, brace._
- Dumbbell bench press or push-up variation: 4 × 5–6 @ RIR 2–3, rest 2–3 min · _Upper-body maintenance; shoulder balance for swimming. Cue: Shoulder blades set, 2 s lower._
- Single-leg RDL (per leg): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Hamstring/glute strength & balance for gait. Cue: Hips square; reach long._
- One-arm dumbbell row (per side): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Pulling strength & posture. Cue: Pull to the hip; no torso twist._
- Step-up to knee drive (per leg): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Running-specific single-leg power. Cue: Drive through the whole foot._
- Copenhagen side plank (per side, seconds): 3 × 20–30 s @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Adductor strength — groin resilience (soccer history). Cue: Start short-lever (knee) and progress._
- Dead bug: 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Deep trunk control. Cue: Low back stays down._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* Heavy but crisp: increase load 2–5% when all sets reach the top of the rep range at RIR ≥2.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).
*Cues:* Quality over grinding: never to failure; Move the weight with intent on the way up; control down; Lift after the endurance session (≥3–6 h later if possible) to protect key endurance quality
*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

### Friday, Dec 11, 2026

**Objective:** Build the ability to swim the full 1.9 km continuously at controlled effort.

#### Endurance 3 × 500 — 2500
Aerobic endurance · Key — mandatory · 51 min · 2500 yd/m · Aerobic endurance

**Why:** You cannot predict a swim split until you can swim the distance comfortably. Longer repeats at aerobic pace build specific endurance and confidence.

*Warm-up*
- 400 easy freestyle, breathing every 3 strokes — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 drill/swim by 25 (catch-up → fingertip drag) — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Long body line; exhale fully underwater._
*Main set*
- 3 × 500 @ CSS +5 to +9 s/100 — 40 s rest; sight every 6–8 strokes on the final 100 of each — CSS +5 to +9 s/100 · RPE 4–6/10 · _Bilateral breathing; sight with "crocodile eyes" then rotate to breathe._
- 4 × 50 descend 1→4 — 15 s rest — RPE 4–8/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool (50 m/long-course if available) or supervised open water — see safety notes. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Settle into rhythm within 100; Long exhale; relaxed kick (2-beat is fine in a wetsuit)
*Success:* Pace stable across repeats; Comfortable enough to continue
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

### Saturday, Dec 12, 2026

**Objective:** Extend aerobic durability and practice race nutrition.

#### Long ride + race-specific blocks — 3 h
Long ride + race-specific blocks · Key — mandatory · 3 h · Z2 with race-power / tempo blocks

**Why:** Long rides develop the fatigue resistance that decides the second half of the bike and the whole run. Race-power blocks late in the ride teach you what 75–80% FTP feels like when you are already tired.

*Warm-up*
- 20 min progressive from easy to endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
*Main set*
- 36 min endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _First fuel at 20 min. Settle in; this is where Waco-style over-pacing starts if you let it._
- 2 × 25 min tempo / race power — 78–85% FTP · HR 88–93% bike LTHR · RPE 5–6/10 · recovery: 10 min endurance between · _Hold steady through rollers — no surges above 95% FTP._
- 54 min endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _Final 10 min: shift to an easier gear, spin 90+ rpm, finish the bottle — prepare legs for the brick._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Rolling route similar to the race (gradual rises, long open roads); stay in aero on safe straights. Trainer if weather or safety requires. Race bike in race position, power meter/HR, race nutrition, saddle bag with tube/CO₂/levers, phone, ID.
*Fueling:* Before: 1–2 g/kg carbohydrate (80–160 g) 2–4 h prior — rehearse your race breakfast. During: 60–90 g/h carbohydrate from glucose + fructose sources (build toward 80–90 g/h by the race-specific phase); 500–800 mL/h fluid adjusted to sweat rate; ~300–600 mg sodium per liter (more if you are a heavy/salty sweater). Log grams per hour and any GI symptoms — this is gut training. Keep eating in the final 20 min of the ride — fuel for the run starts on the bike. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Eat every 20 min, drink every 10–15 min; In aero: chin down, shoulders relaxed, elbows narrow; On rises: shift early, hold power, do not chase speed; On descents: keep light pressure, do not coast if speed is low
*Success:* Normalized power ≤ 1.05 × average power (Variability Index ≤1.05) on steady segments; Hit fueling target (log grams/hour); Blocks within target; final hour not slower than the first
*Modifications:* fatigue: Remove the blocks and ride 70–80% of the duration in Z2. If fatigue is high for 2+ days, ride 60–75 min easy instead. | weather: Rain, ice, lightning, or wind gusts >25 mph: ride indoors at 80–90% duration (indoor riding is harder per minute — continuous pedaling, more heat). | time: Minimum 75% of duration with all blocks intact; trim endurance riding first. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Brick run — 20 min
Brick run · Supporting — mandatory · 20 min · Z2

**Why:** Running economy is impaired immediately after cycling (Millet & Vleck 2000). Regular brick runs train the cycle-to-run transition and teach you to judge race pace when your legs lie to you.

*Warm-up*
- T2 practice: rack bike, helmet off, shoes on, go — target <2 min — RPE 3/10
*Main set*
- 20 min steady aerobic off the bike — 13% slower to 22% slower than threshold pace · HR 85–89% run LTHR · RPE 3–4/10 · _Focus on cadence (≈170–180 spm) in the first 5 min._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min walk — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat loop starting where you finish the ride. Race shoes with elastic laces, race belt, visor, gel.
*Fueling:* Take a gel or 20–25 g carbohydrate in the first 10 min if the run is ≥30 min (practice race-day timing). Drink at a "simulated aid station" every ~1 mile/10 min.
*Cues:* Short quick steps for the first 5 min; Pace by HR/RPE first, then the watch; Note how race pace feels — this calibrates race day
*Success:* Hit pace without exceeding the HR ceiling; Transition <2 min; Log RPE and how legs felt at 5 min vs. 15 min
*Modifications:* fatigue: Run 10 min easy only, or walk 10 min. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: 10 min minimum — the transition itself is the stimulus. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Ecclesiastes 9:10** (Diligence) — Whatever your hand finds to do, do it with all your might.
>
> A three-hour ride deserves your full presence — not distracted, not drifting into surges.
>
> _Be fully present for the whole ride._

### Sunday, Dec 13, 2026

**Objective:** Extend running durability so pace holds in the final 5K of the race.

#### Long run — 85 min
Long run · Key — mandatory · 1 h 25 min · Z2 aerobic

**Why:** The 70.3 run is decided by durability: holding form and pace after 3+ hours of racing. Long runs build musculoskeletal resilience and fat oxidation; race-pace segments late in the run rehearse running well while fatigued.

*Warm-up*
- 5 min walk → easy jog — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Main set*
- 80 min easy aerobic — 13% slower to 22% slower than threshold pace · HR 85–89% run LTHR · RPE 3–4/10 · _First 10 min easier than you think — practice the patience you need on race day._
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat-to-gently-rolling, ideally crushed gravel/trail (race surface). Loop past your car or a water fountain for fluids. Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals). Handheld/belt with fluids and gels for runs >75 min.
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Run the hills by effort, not pace; Gel practice at the times you will take them in the race; Check form every 10 min: tall, relaxed, quick
*Success:* Pace in the final 20 min ≥ the first 20 min at similar effort; HR drift <5% in the easy portion (where pace is steady); No pain that alters gait
*Modifications:* fatigue: Run 60–70% of the duration, all easy, no race-pace blocks. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep at least 75% of the duration; race-pace blocks stay. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Psalm 37:23–24** (Trust) — The Lord makes firm the steps of those who delight in him; though they stumble, they will not fall, for the Lord upholds them.
>
> Long runs include stumbles — a bad mile, a heavy leg. You will not be cast down.
>
> _Delight in God on the run today._

## Week 10 — Base 2 — week 3 (block peak) (Mon Dec 14 – Sun Dec 20)

**Phase:** Bike power & climbing / run durability · **Type:** build · **Planned:** 11 h 25 min

First sustained race-power work; first race-pace brick.

### Monday, Dec 14, 2026

**Objective:** Rest and recover — absorb the training.

#### Rest day
Rest · Key — mandatory · — · Rest

**Why:** Planned rest prevents the slow slide into fatigue that ruins key sessions. Sleep 7–9 h, eat normally (do not "save calories" on rest days), walk if you like.

*Main set*
- No structured training. Optional: 20–30 min easy walk outdoors. — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* — —
*Fueling:* Carbohydrate ≈3–5 g/kg (250–415 g at 83 kg); protein ≈1.6–2.0 g/kg (135–165 g) spread over 4–5 meals. Hydrate normally; pale-yellow urine is a reasonable everyday check.
*Cues:* Bedtime 30 min earlier than usual; Fill in the daily check-in (sleep, fatigue, soreness)
*Success:* You actually rested
*Modifications:* illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Mobility & tissue care — 20 min
Mobility · Optional · 20 min · Recovery

**Why:** Hip-flexor, thoracic, and ankle mobility support an aero position you can hold and a relaxed stride. Low cost, high return on a rest day.

*Main set*
- 90/90 hip switches — 2 × 8 per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Half-kneeling hip-flexor stretch with reach — 2 × 45 s per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Thoracic open-books — 2 × 8 per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Knee-to-wall ankle mobilisation — 2 × 10 per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Band shoulder dislocates + wall slides — 2 × 10 — RPE 1–2/10
- Calf/soleus stretch (straight and bent knee) — 2 × 45 s per side — RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Home Mat, band, optional foam roller.
*Fueling:* Rest days still need carbohydrate (≈3–5 g/kg) and protein (≈1.6–2.0 g/kg) — recovery happens today.
*Cues:* Slow breathing; no pain
*Success:* Done, and you feel looser
*Modifications:* fatigue: Optional — skip if you need sleep more. | time: 10 min version: first three exercises. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> Some things are too great for you to control: the weather on March 14, the competition, the outcome. Calm your soul.

### Tuesday, Dec 15, 2026

**Objective:** Lift FTP so race power (75–80% FTP) becomes a smaller fraction of your ceiling.

#### Threshold 2 × 20 min — 75 min
Threshold intervals · Key — mandatory · 1 h 15 min · Z4 threshold (95–100% FTP)

**Why:** Raising FTP is the most direct way to ride faster at the same relative effort. If FTP rises 5%, the same 77% race intensity is ~5% more watts — roughly 3–5 minutes over 56 miles — without costing your run.

*Warm-up*
- 10 min progressive spin — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _Start at RPE 2 and finish at the top of endurance._
- 3 × 1 min high-cadence spin-ups (100–110 rpm) / 1 min easy — RPE 3–4/10 · _Smooth pedal stroke; no bouncing in the saddle._
- 3 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Main set*
- 2 × 20 min at threshold — 95–100% FTP · HR 95–100% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 7–8/10 · recovery: 6 min easy spin · _Settle into the range within 60 s; do not start above it._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy spin, cadence 90+ — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Trainer preferred for control; or a flat/steady road without stops. Smart trainer or quiet road loop; power meter if available, HR strap, two bottles, fan indoors.
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Even pacing across reps; Relax the upper body; tension wastes energy
*Success:* ≥90% of interval time within range; Final rep completed without dropping >5%
*Modifications:* fatigue: If sleep <6 h, fatigue ≥7/10 or the first rep is a struggle at the low end of the target: drop one rep per set and cap effort at the bottom of the range. If two reps in a row miss by >5%, stop and ride Z2 — that is data, not failure. | weather: Do it indoors. Outdoor wind makes steady power hard; use a flat, safe loop if you must ride outside. | time: Keep warm-up to 12 min and complete two-thirds of the work; do not rush the recoveries. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength A — lower-body pull + upper pull — Strength development
Strength · Supporting — mandatory · 58 min · RIR 2–3 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021).

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Trap-bar deadlift (or barbell RDL if no trap bar): 4 × 5–6 @ RIR 2–3, rest 2–3 min · _Posterior-chain force for climbing and run propulsion. Cue: Brace, push the floor away, neutral spine._
- Rear-foot-elevated split squat (per leg): 4 × 5–6 @ RIR 2–3, rest 2–3 min · _Single-leg strength and hip stability for running. Cue: Front shin near vertical, control the descent 2–3 s._
- Pull-up / chin-up (assisted if needed): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Upper-back strength for swim catch and aero posture. Cue: Full hang to chin over bar; ribs down._
- Standing calf raise — slow (3 s down): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Gastrocnemius strength/Achilles resilience. Cue: Full range; pause at the top._
- Bent-knee (seated) soleus raise: 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Soleus takes the highest loads in running. Cue: Knee bent ~90°, heavy load, slow._
- Pallof press (per side): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Anti-rotation trunk control for run stability and aero bars. Cue: Press out, hold 2 s, resist rotation._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* Heavy but crisp: increase load 2–5% when all sets reach the top of the rep range at RIR ≥2.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).
*Cues:* Quality over grinding: never to failure; Move the weight with intent on the way up; control down; Lift after the endurance session (≥3–6 h later if possible) to protect key endurance quality
*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **2 Timothy 1:7** (Courage) — God did not give us a spirit of fear, but of power, love, and self-discipline.
>
> Twenty minutes at threshold — twice. Power and self-discipline together: hard enough to grow, controlled enough to finish.
>
> _Ride both reps with self-discipline._

### Wednesday, Dec 16, 2026

**Objective:** Raise sustainable swim speed (threshold).

#### CSS set 15 × 100 — 2500
Threshold (CSS) · Key — mandatory · 51 min · 2500 yd/m · CSS / threshold

**Why:** CSS work is the most time-efficient way for age-group triathletes to raise sustainable swim pace. A faster CSS means 70.3 race effort (CSS +3–6 s/100) costs less, so you exit the water fresher.

*Warm-up*
- 400 easy freestyle, breathing every 3 strokes — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 drill/swim by 25 (catch-up → fingertip drag) — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Long body line; exhale fully underwater._
*Main set*
- 15 × 100 @ CSS +0 to +2 s/100 — 10 s rest — CSS +0 to +2 s/100 · RPE 6–8/10 · _Even pace. Use the pace clock; stroke count steady._
- 200 pull with buoy, easy-moderate — CSS +5 to +9 s/100 · RPE 4–5/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Hold the same pace on every rep — the last one should not be the slowest; Rotate to breathe, do not lift the head
*Success:* ≥80% of reps within target; Stroke count rose ≤2 per length late in the set
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Easy aerobic run — 40 min
Easy run · Supporting — mandatory · 40 min · Z1–Z2

**Why:** Easy running volume strengthens tendons, bones, and aerobic economy — the foundation for running well off the bike. Keeping easy runs truly easy preserves energy for the sessions that matter (Seiler 2010).

*Main set*
- 40 min easy continuous — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10 · _Easy means you could hold a full conversation. Walk breaks on hills are fine if HR rises above target._
*Terrain/equipment:* Soft or mixed surfaces; include crushed-gravel trail when possible (race surface). Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required.
*Cues:* Cadence quick and light (no overstriding); Relaxed hands and jaw; Nose-breathing test: if you cannot, slow down
*Success:* HR stayed under the Z2 ceiling (or RPE ≤3); Finished feeling you could do more
*Modifications:* fatigue: Replace with 30 min walk or bike spin; easy runs are the first sessions to cut when tired. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Galatians 5:22–23** (Self-control) — The fruit of the Spirit includes love, joy, peace, patience, kindness, goodness, faithfulness, gentleness, and self-control.
>
> Fifteen 100s on short rest test patience and self-control. These are fruit — grown, not forced.
>
> _Which fruit of the Spirit do I most need today?_

### Thursday, Dec 17, 2026

**Objective:** Raise lactate threshold / critical speed — the ceiling under your 70.3 run pace.

#### Threshold cruise intervals 3 × 10 min — 61 min
Threshold intervals · Key — mandatory · 1 h 01 min · Z4 threshold

**Why:** Threshold work raises the speed you can sustain before fatigue accelerates (critical speed; Jones & Vanhatalo 2017). Your off-the-bike half-marathon pace is a fraction of it, so a higher ceiling makes race pace easier.

*Warm-up*
- 10 min easy jog — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
- Drills: 2 × 20 m each — A-skips, high knees, butt kicks, carioca — RPE 3–4/10
- 4 × 20 s strides / 60–90 s walk-jog — RPE 6–7/10 · _Fast and relaxed — about 5K effort, not a sprint. Tall posture, quick light feet._
*Main set*
- 3 × 10 min at threshold — 1% faster to 3% slower than threshold pace · HR 95–99% run LTHR · RPE 7–8/10 · recovery: 120 s easy jog · _"Comfortably hard": controlled, rhythmic breathing; the last rep should be the best one._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy jog + 5 min walk — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat loop, track, or crushed-gravel trail; treadmill at 1% acceptable. Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Even splits — the first rep is where pacing discipline is built; Relax shoulders on every lap
*Success:* All reps within the pace/HR range; Last rep ≤3 s/mi slower than the first
*Modifications:* fatigue: If fatigue ≥7/10, soreness ≥6/10 or sleep <6 h: run easy for 60–70% of the time, no intervals. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength B — squat + upper push + single-leg — Strength development
Strength · Supporting — mandatory · 1 h 05 min · RIR 2–3 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021).

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Back squat or goblet/safety-bar squat: 4 × 5–6 @ RIR 2–3, rest 2–3 min · _Quad & glute strength for bike force and descents. Cue: Controlled depth you own, brace._
- Dumbbell bench press or push-up variation: 4 × 5–6 @ RIR 2–3, rest 2–3 min · _Upper-body maintenance; shoulder balance for swimming. Cue: Shoulder blades set, 2 s lower._
- Single-leg RDL (per leg): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Hamstring/glute strength & balance for gait. Cue: Hips square; reach long._
- One-arm dumbbell row (per side): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Pulling strength & posture. Cue: Pull to the hip; no torso twist._
- Step-up to knee drive (per leg): 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Running-specific single-leg power. Cue: Drive through the whole foot._
- Copenhagen side plank (per side, seconds): 3 × 20–30 s @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Adductor strength — groin resilience (soccer history). Cue: Start short-lever (knee) and progress._
- Dead bug: 3 × 6–10 @ RIR 2–3, rest 90 s · _Deep trunk control. Cue: Low back stays down._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* Heavy but crisp: increase load 2–5% when all sets reach the top of the rep range at RIR ≥2.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).
*Cues:* Quality over grinding: never to failure; Move the weight with intent on the way up; control down; Lift after the endurance session (≥3–6 h later if possible) to protect key endurance quality
*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Proverbs 19:2** (Pacing) — Desire without knowledge is not good, and whoever hurries with his feet misses the way.
>
> This might be the most "Waco" verse in Scripture: hurrying feet miss the way. Run the first cruise interval with knowledge, not just desire.

### Friday, Dec 18, 2026

**Objective:** Make open-water navigation, starts, drafting, and turns automatic.

#### Open-water skills (pool) — 2200
Open-water skills · Supporting — mandatory · 45 min · 2200 yd/m · Race effort + skills

**Why:** Poor sighting adds distance; a panicked start wrecks the first 300. These skills are rehearsable in a pool and directly reduce your swim time and stress.

*Warm-up*
- 400 easy freestyle, breathing every 3 strokes — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 drill/swim by 25 (catch-up → fingertip drag) — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Long body line; exhale fully underwater._
*Main set*
- 4 × 100 sighting: lift eyes forward every 6 strokes, then every 4 — 15 s rest — CSS +5 to +9 s/100 · RPE 4–5/10 · _Pick a target on the far wall; eyes only just above water, then roll to breathe._
- 6 × 50 "race start": first 15 strokes fast, then settle to race effort — 20 s rest — RPE 5–8/10 · _Rehearse settling breathing after the surge — the critical skill for a calm start._
- 4 × 100 drafting: swim on a partner's feet or hip (or sight on the lane line) — 15 s rest — CSS +3 to +6 s/100 · RPE 5–6/10 · _Legal in age-group swim. Without a partner: practise swimming straight with eyes closed for 6 strokes, then sight._
- 1 × 300 continuous at race effort with mid-pool "buoy turn" (no wall push) each 100 — 30 s rest — CSS +3 to +6 s/100 · RPE 5–6/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool (50 m/long-course if available) or supervised open water — see safety notes. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Sight → breathe in one fluid motion; Turn at "buoys" by rolling onto your back briefly or a tight corkscrew
*Success:* Sighting without stopping the stroke; Calm breathing within 30 s of each start surge
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Matthew 14:22–33** (Faith in uncertainty) — Peter walks on the water toward Jesus but begins to sink when he sees the wind; Jesus catches him immediately.
>
> Sighting practice: where you look determines where you go. Peter sank when he looked at the wind instead of Jesus.
>
> _Where are my eyes fixed this week?_

### Saturday, Dec 19, 2026

**Objective:** Extend aerobic durability and practice race nutrition.

#### Long ride + race-specific blocks — 3 h
Long ride + race-specific blocks · Key — mandatory · 3 h · Z2 with race-power / tempo blocks

**Why:** Long rides develop the fatigue resistance that decides the second half of the bike and the whole run. Race-power blocks late in the ride teach you what 75–80% FTP feels like when you are already tired.

*Warm-up*
- 20 min progressive from easy to endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
*Main set*
- 32 min endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _First fuel at 20 min. Settle in; this is where Waco-style over-pacing starts if you let it._
- 3 × 20 min race power — 75–80% FTP · HR 86–92% bike LTHR · Cadence 82–92 rpm · RPE 5–6/10 · recovery: 5 min endurance between
- 48 min endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _Final 10 min: shift to an easier gear, spin 90+ rpm, finish the bottle — prepare legs for the brick._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Rolling route similar to the race (gradual rises, long open roads); stay in aero on safe straights. Trainer if weather or safety requires. Race bike in race position, power meter/HR, race nutrition, saddle bag with tube/CO₂/levers, phone, ID.
*Fueling:* Before: 1–2 g/kg carbohydrate (80–160 g) 2–4 h prior — rehearse your race breakfast. During: 60–90 g/h carbohydrate from glucose + fructose sources (build toward 80–90 g/h by the race-specific phase); 500–800 mL/h fluid adjusted to sweat rate; ~300–600 mg sodium per liter (more if you are a heavy/salty sweater). Log grams per hour and any GI symptoms — this is gut training. Keep eating in the final 20 min of the ride — fuel for the run starts on the bike. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Eat every 20 min, drink every 10–15 min; In aero: chin down, shoulders relaxed, elbows narrow; On rises: shift early, hold power, do not chase speed; On descents: keep light pressure, do not coast if speed is low
*Success:* Normalized power ≤ 1.05 × average power (Variability Index ≤1.05) on steady segments; Hit fueling target (log grams/hour); Blocks within target; final hour not slower than the first
*Modifications:* fatigue: Remove the blocks and ride 70–80% of the duration in Z2. If fatigue is high for 2+ days, ride 60–75 min easy instead. | weather: Rain, ice, lightning, or wind gusts >25 mph: ride indoors at 80–90% duration (indoor riding is harder per minute — continuous pedaling, more heat). | time: Minimum 75% of duration with all blocks intact; trim endurance riding first. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Brick run — 20 min (10 min race pace)
Brick run · Key — mandatory · 20 min · 70.3 race pace

**Why:** Running economy is impaired immediately after cycling (Millet & Vleck 2000). Regular brick runs train the cycle-to-run transition and teach you to judge race pace when your legs lie to you.

*Warm-up*
- T2 practice: rack bike, helmet off, shoes on, go — target <2 min — RPE 3/10
*Main set*
- First 10 min at 70.3 race pace — 6% slower to 11% slower than threshold pace · HR 89–93% run LTHR · RPE 5–6/10 · _First 3–5 min: quick, short steps; let the legs come around. Do not chase pace in mile 1 — that is the Waco lesson._
- 10 min easy — 13% slower to 22% slower than threshold pace · HR 85–89% run LTHR · RPE 3–4/10
*Cool-down*
- 5 min walk — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat loop starting where you finish the ride. Race shoes with elastic laces, race belt, visor, gel.
*Fueling:* Take a gel or 20–25 g carbohydrate in the first 10 min if the run is ≥30 min (practice race-day timing). Drink at a "simulated aid station" every ~1 mile/10 min.
*Cues:* Short quick steps for the first 5 min; Pace by HR/RPE first, then the watch; Note how race pace feels — this calibrates race day
*Success:* Hit pace without exceeding the HR ceiling; Transition <2 min; Log RPE and how legs felt at 5 min vs. 15 min
*Modifications:* fatigue: Run 10 min easy only, or walk 10 min. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: 10 min minimum — the transition itself is the stimulus. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Luke 14:28–30** (Planning) — Jesus says anyone building a tower first sits down to estimate the cost, so they can finish.
>
> Race power on today's long ride is counting the cost: can you afford this effort and still run? Plan the spend.
>
> _Finish the brick knowing you could run more._

### Sunday, Dec 20, 2026

**Objective:** Extend running durability so pace holds in the final 5K of the race.

#### Long run — 90 min
Long run · Key — mandatory · 1 h 30 min · Z2 aerobic

**Why:** The 70.3 run is decided by durability: holding form and pace after 3+ hours of racing. Long runs build musculoskeletal resilience and fat oxidation; race-pace segments late in the run rehearse running well while fatigued.

*Warm-up*
- 5 min walk → easy jog — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Main set*
- 85 min easy aerobic — 13% slower to 22% slower than threshold pace · HR 85–89% run LTHR · RPE 3–4/10 · _First 10 min easier than you think — practice the patience you need on race day._
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat-to-gently-rolling, ideally crushed gravel/trail (race surface). Loop past your car or a water fountain for fluids. Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals). Handheld/belt with fluids and gels for runs >75 min.
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Run the hills by effort, not pace; Gel practice at the times you will take them in the race; Check form every 10 min: tall, relaxed, quick
*Success:* Pace in the final 20 min ≥ the first 20 min at similar effort; HR drift <5% in the easy portion (where pace is steady); No pain that alters gait
*Modifications:* fatigue: Run 60–70% of the duration, all easy, no race-pace blocks. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep at least 75% of the duration; race-pace blocks stay. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.
