### Wednesday, Jan 13, 2027

**Objective:** Build the ability to swim the full 1.9 km continuously at controlled effort.

#### Endurance 3 × 600 — 2800
Aerobic endurance · Key — mandatory · 57 min · 2800 yd/m · Aerobic endurance

**Why:** You cannot predict a swim split until you can swim the distance comfortably. Longer repeats at aerobic pace build specific endurance and confidence.

*Warm-up*
- 400 easy freestyle, breathing every 3 strokes — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 drill/swim by 25 (catch-up → fingertip drag) — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Long body line; exhale fully underwater._
*Main set*
- 3 × 600 @ CSS +4 to +6 s/100 — 40 s rest; sight every 6–8 strokes on the final 100 of each — CSS +4 to +6 s/100 · RPE 4–6/10 · _Bilateral breathing; sight with "crocodile eyes" then rotate to breathe._
- 4 × 50 descend 1→4 — 15 s rest — RPE 4–8/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool (50 m/long-course if available) or supervised open water — see safety notes. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Settle into rhythm within 100; Long exhale; relaxed kick (2-beat is fine in a wetsuit)
*Success:* Pace stable across repeats; Comfortable enough to continue
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Easy aerobic run — 40 min
Easy run · Supporting — mandatory · 40 min · Z1–Z2

**Why:** Easy running volume strengthens tendons, bones, and aerobic economy — the foundation for running well off the bike. Keeping easy runs truly easy preserves energy for the sessions that matter (Seiler 2010).

*Main set*
- 40 min easy continuous — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10 · _Easy means you could hold a full conversation. Walk breaks on hills are fine if HR rises above target._
*Terrain/equipment:* Soft or mixed surfaces; include crushed-gravel trail when possible (race surface). Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required.
*Cues:* Cadence quick and light (no overstriding); Relaxed hands and jaw; Nose-breathing test: if you cannot, slow down
*Success:* HR stayed under the Z2 ceiling (or RPE ≤3); Finished feeling you could do more
*Modifications:* fatigue: Replace with 30 min walk or bike spin; easy runs are the first sessions to cut when tired. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Psalm 29:3–4, 10–11** (Trust) — The voice of the Lord is over the waters; he sits enthroned over the flood; he gives strength to his people and blesses them with peace.
>
> Strength and peace together — the goal of every endurance swim.
>
> _Swim with strength and peace._

### Thursday, Jan 14, 2027

**Objective:** Raise lactate threshold / critical speed — the ceiling under your 70.3 run pace.

#### Threshold cruise intervals 3 × 12 min — 67 min
Threshold intervals · Key — mandatory · 1 h 07 min · Z4 threshold

**Why:** Threshold work raises the speed you can sustain before fatigue accelerates (critical speed; Jones & Vanhatalo 2017). Your off-the-bike half-marathon pace is a fraction of it, so a higher ceiling makes race pace easier.

*Warm-up*
- 10 min easy jog — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
- Drills: 2 × 20 m each — A-skips, high knees, butt kicks, carioca — RPE 3–4/10
- 4 × 20 s strides / 60–90 s walk-jog — RPE 6–7/10 · _Fast and relaxed — about 5K effort, not a sprint. Tall posture, quick light feet._
*Main set*
- 3 × 12 min at threshold — 1% faster to 3% slower than threshold pace · HR 95–99% run LTHR · RPE 7–8/10 · recovery: 120 s easy jog · _"Comfortably hard": controlled, rhythmic breathing; the last rep should be the best one._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy jog + 5 min walk — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat loop, track, or crushed-gravel trail; treadmill at 1% acceptable. Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Even splits — the first rep is where pacing discipline is built; Relax shoulders on every lap
*Success:* All reps within the pace/HR range; Last rep ≤3 s/mi slower than the first
*Modifications:* fatigue: If fatigue ≥7/10, soreness ≥6/10 or sleep <6 h: run easy for 60–70% of the time, no intervals. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength B — squat + upper push + single-leg — Strength maintenance (heavy, lower volume)
Strength · Supporting — mandatory · 48 min · RIR 2–3 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021).

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Back squat or goblet/safety-bar squat: 3 × 4–5 @ RIR 2–3, rest 2–3 min · _Quad & glute strength for bike force and descents. Cue: Controlled depth you own, brace._
- Dumbbell bench press or push-up variation: 3 × 4–5 @ RIR 2–3, rest 2–3 min · _Upper-body maintenance; shoulder balance for swimming. Cue: Shoulder blades set, 2 s lower._
- Single-leg RDL (per leg): 2 × 6–8 @ RIR 3, rest 90 s · _Hamstring/glute strength & balance for gait. Cue: Hips square; reach long._
- One-arm dumbbell row (per side): 2 × 6–8 @ RIR 3, rest 90 s · _Pulling strength & posture. Cue: Pull to the hip; no torso twist._
- Step-up to knee drive (per leg): 2 × 6–8 @ RIR 3, rest 90 s · _Running-specific single-leg power. Cue: Drive through the whole foot._
- Copenhagen side plank (per side, seconds): 2 × 20–30 s @ RIR 3, rest 90 s · _Adductor strength — groin resilience (soccer history). Cue: Start short-lever (knee) and progress._
- Dead bug: 2 × 6–8 @ RIR 3, rest 90 s · _Deep trunk control. Cue: Low back stays down._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* Hold last block's loads — maintaining intensity preserves strength while volume falls.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).
*Cues:* Quality over grinding: never to failure; Move the weight with intent on the way up; control down; Lift after the endurance session (≥3–6 h later if possible) to protect key endurance quality
*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Romans 12:1–2** (Stewardship) — Offer your bodies as living sacrifices, holy and pleasing to God; be transformed by the renewing of your mind.
>
> Training is a bodily act of worship when it is offered to God. Threshold reps can be a sacrifice of praise.
>
> _Offer today's session to God before you start._

### Friday, Jan 15, 2027

**Objective:** Improve body position, catch, and breathing rhythm — free speed through efficiency.

#### Technique & feel — 2600
Technique · Supporting — mandatory · 53 min · 2600 yd/m · Easy / aerobic with technique focus

**Why:** Swim speed in age-groupers is limited more by technique (drag, catch) than by fitness. Efficient strokes save energy for the bike and run.

*Warm-up*
- 400 easy freestyle, breathing every 3 strokes — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 drill/swim by 25 (catch-up → fingertip drag) — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Long body line; exhale fully underwater._
*Main set*
- 6 × 50 kick on side with fins (25 each side) — 15 s rest — RPE 3–4/10 · _Head in line, bottom arm extended, hips at the surface._
- 6 × 50 "6-kick switch" drill — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Six kicks on side, one stroke, rotate — teaches rotation and balance._
- 11 × 100 swim, focusing on one cue per 100 (high elbow catch → push past hip → exhale) — 15 s rest — CSS +5 to +9 s/100 · RPE 4–5/10
- 4 × 25 fast with perfect form — 30 s rest — CSS -6 to -2 s/100 · RPE 8–9/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Press the chest down, hips up; Reach long, then catch with the forearm vertical; Exhale steadily underwater; quick inhale
*Success:* Stroke count per length stayed the same or dropped across the 100s; Felt smoother by the end
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Proverbs 15:22** (Counsel) — Plans fail for lack of counsel, but with many advisers they succeed.
>
> Technique improves with outside eyes. Consider asking a coach or friend to film your stroke.
>
> _Seek one piece of counsel this week._

### Saturday, Jan 16, 2027

**Objective:** Extend aerobic durability and practice race nutrition.

#### Long ride + race-specific blocks — 3:30 h
Long ride + race-specific blocks · Key — mandatory · 3 h 30 min · Z2 with race-power / tempo blocks

**Why:** Long rides develop the fatigue resistance that decides the second half of the bike and the whole run. Race-power blocks late in the ride teach you what 75–80% FTP feels like when you are already tired.

*Warm-up*
- 20 min progressive from easy to endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
*Main set*
- 38 min endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _First fuel at 20 min. Settle in; this is where Waco-style over-pacing starts if you let it._
- 3 × 25 min race power — 75–80% FTP · HR 86–92% bike LTHR · Cadence 82–92 rpm · RPE 5–6/10 · recovery: 5 min endurance between
- 57 min endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _Final 10 min: shift to an easier gear, spin 90+ rpm, finish the bottle — prepare legs for the brick._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Rolling route similar to the race (gradual rises, long open roads); stay in aero on safe straights. Trainer if weather or safety requires. Race bike in race position, power meter/HR, race nutrition, saddle bag with tube/CO₂/levers, phone, ID.
*Fueling:* Before: 1–2 g/kg carbohydrate (80–160 g) 2–4 h prior — rehearse your race breakfast. During: 60–90 g/h carbohydrate from glucose + fructose sources (build toward 80–90 g/h by the race-specific phase); 500–800 mL/h fluid adjusted to sweat rate; ~300–600 mg sodium per liter (more if you are a heavy/salty sweater). Log grams per hour and any GI symptoms — this is gut training. Keep eating in the final 20 min of the ride — fuel for the run starts on the bike. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Eat every 20 min, drink every 10–15 min; In aero: chin down, shoulders relaxed, elbows narrow; On rises: shift early, hold power, do not chase speed; On descents: keep light pressure, do not coast if speed is low
*Success:* Normalized power ≤ 1.05 × average power (Variability Index ≤1.05) on steady segments; Hit fueling target (log grams/hour); Blocks within target; final hour not slower than the first
*Modifications:* fatigue: Remove the blocks and ride 70–80% of the duration in Z2. If fatigue is high for 2+ days, ride 60–75 min easy instead. | weather: Rain, ice, lightning, or wind gusts >25 mph: ride indoors at 80–90% duration (indoor riding is harder per minute — continuous pedaling, more heat). | time: Minimum 75% of duration with all blocks intact; trim endurance riding first. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Brick run — 30 min (20 min race pace)
Brick run · Key — mandatory · 30 min · 70.3 race pace

**Why:** Running economy is impaired immediately after cycling (Millet & Vleck 2000). Regular brick runs train the cycle-to-run transition and teach you to judge race pace when your legs lie to you.

*Warm-up*
- T2 practice: rack bike, helmet off, shoes on, go — target <2 min — RPE 3/10
*Main set*
- First 20 min at 70.3 race pace — 6% slower to 11% slower than threshold pace · HR 89–93% run LTHR · RPE 5–6/10 · _First 3–5 min: quick, short steps; let the legs come around. Do not chase pace in mile 1 — that is the Waco lesson._
- 10 min easy — 13% slower to 22% slower than threshold pace · HR 85–89% run LTHR · RPE 3–4/10
*Cool-down*
- 5 min walk — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat loop starting where you finish the ride. Race shoes with elastic laces, race belt, visor, gel.
*Fueling:* Take a gel or 20–25 g carbohydrate in the first 10 min if the run is ≥30 min (practice race-day timing). Drink at a "simulated aid station" every ~1 mile/10 min.
*Cues:* Short quick steps for the first 5 min; Pace by HR/RPE first, then the watch; Note how race pace feels — this calibrates race day
*Success:* Hit pace without exceeding the HR ceiling; Transition <2 min; Log RPE and how legs felt at 5 min vs. 15 min
*Modifications:* fatigue: Run 10 min easy only, or walk 10 min. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: 10 min minimum — the transition itself is the stimulus. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Hebrews 10:35–36** (Perseverance) — Do not throw away your confidence; you need to persevere so that when you have done the will of God, you will receive what he has promised.
>
> 75 minutes of race power plus a race-pace brick: the biggest day yet. "You need to persevere" — the writer says it plainly.
>
> _Keep your confidence through the final block._

### Sunday, Jan 17, 2027

**Objective:** Extend running durability so pace holds in the final 5K of the race.

#### Long run — 100 min with race-pace work
Long run · Key — mandatory · 1 h 40 min · Z2 + 70.3 race pace

**Why:** The 70.3 run is decided by durability: holding form and pace after 3+ hours of racing. Long runs build musculoskeletal resilience and fat oxidation; race-pace segments late in the run rehearse running well while fatigued.

*Warm-up*
- 5 min walk → easy jog — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Main set*
- 39 min easy aerobic — 13% slower to 22% slower than threshold pace · HR 85–89% run LTHR · RPE 3–4/10 · _First 10 min easier than you think — practice the patience you need on race day._
- 2 × 15 min at 70.3 race pace — 6% slower to 11% slower than threshold pace · HR 89–93% run LTHR · RPE 5–6/10 · recovery: 5 min easy jog
- 21 min easy — 13% slower to 22% slower than threshold pace · HR 85–89% run LTHR · RPE 3–4/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat-to-gently-rolling, ideally crushed gravel/trail (race surface). Loop past your car or a water fountain for fluids. Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals). Handheld/belt with fluids and gels for runs >75 min.
*Fueling:* Before: 1–2 g/kg carbohydrate (80–160 g) 2–3 h prior; familiar, low-fiber foods. During: 45–60 g/h carbohydrate starting at 20 min; 500–750 mL/h fluid adjusted to sweat rate and weather; ~300–600 mg sodium per liter. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Run the hills by effort, not pace; Gel practice at the times you will take them in the race; Check form every 10 min: tall, relaxed, quick
*Success:* Pace in the final 20 min ≥ the first 20 min at similar effort; HR drift <5% in the easy portion (where pace is steady); No pain that alters gait
*Modifications:* fatigue: Run 60–70% of the duration, all easy, no race-pace blocks. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep at least 75% of the duration; race-pace blocks stay. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

## Week 15 — Recovery + full retest (Mon Jan 18 – Sun Jan 24)

**Phase:** Build — threshold & race-specific endurance · **Type:** recovery · **Planned:** 6 h 26 min

CSS (Wed), run threshold (Thu), FTP (Sat). Mid-plan performance review.

**Deload:** volume — ≈46% less than Week 14 (11.9 h → 6.4 h). Build 1 carried the plan's first high-intensity density; a full test battery needs fresh legs. Kept — Three benchmark tests and short strides; one CSS touch through the test itself. Removed — Long ride → FTP test + easy brick; long run → 70 min easy; VO₂ work removed. Lifting — Two deload sessions — same loads (~90%), sets halved — then re-test e1RM next week only if fresh. Monitor — Compare morning HR, sleep, and the "legs" feeling to Week 7 and 11. Persisting heaviness → extend recovery 2–3 days before Build 2.

### Monday, Jan 18, 2027

**Objective:** Rest and recover — absorb the training.

#### Rest day
Rest · Key — mandatory · — · Rest

**Why:** Planned rest prevents the slow slide into fatigue that ruins key sessions. Sleep 7–9 h, eat normally (do not "save calories" on rest days), walk if you like.

*Main set*
- No structured training. Optional: 20–30 min easy walk outdoors. — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* — —
*Fueling:* Carbohydrate ≈3–5 g/kg (250–415 g at 83 kg); protein ≈1.6–2.0 g/kg (135–165 g) spread over 4–5 meals. Hydrate normally; pale-yellow urine is a reasonable everyday check.
*Cues:* Bedtime 30 min earlier than usual; Fill in the daily check-in (sleep, fatigue, soreness)
*Success:* You actually rested
*Modifications:* illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

### Tuesday, Jan 19, 2027

**Objective:** Build aerobic capacity and pedaling efficiency at low physiological cost.

#### Aerobic endurance ride — 50 min
Endurance + skills · Supporting — mandatory · 50 min · Z2 (endurance)

**Why:** Most of a 70.3 bike is ridden in the aerobic domain. Low-intensity volume builds the engine (mitochondrial and capillary adaptations) that lets you hold race power with less fatigue.

*Warm-up*
- 10 min progressive — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
*Main set*
- 23 min steady endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
- 6 × 1 min fast cadence (105–115 rpm) / 1 min normal cadence — RPE 3–5/10 · _Builds neuromuscular smoothness — stay seated and quiet through the hips._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat to gently rolling; trainer fine. Smart trainer or quiet road loop; power meter if available, HR strap, two bottles, fan indoors.
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required.
*Cues:* Conversational effort the whole time; Smooth circles; no mashing on small rises
*Success:* Power/HR stayed in Z2 for ≥90% of the main set; HR drift small on a steady effort
*Modifications:* fatigue: Cut to 30–40 min all Z1–Z2, skip drills. | weather: Trainer. | time: Minimum 40 min; keep the drill block, trim steady riding. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength A — lower-body pull + upper pull — Deload
Strength · Supporting — mandatory · 29 min · RIR 4 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021).

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Trap-bar deadlift (or barbell RDL if no trap bar): 2 × 5 @ RIR 4, rest 2 min · _Posterior-chain force for climbing and run propulsion. Cue: Brace, push the floor away, neutral spine._
- Rear-foot-elevated split squat (per leg): 2 × 5 @ RIR 4, rest 2 min · _Single-leg strength and hip stability for running. Cue: Front shin near vertical, control the descent 2–3 s._
- Pull-up / chin-up (assisted if needed): 1 × 8 @ RIR 4, rest 60 s · _Upper-back strength for swim catch and aero posture. Cue: Full hang to chin over bar; ribs down._
- Standing calf raise — slow (3 s down): 1 × 8 @ RIR 4, rest 60 s · _Gastrocnemius strength/Achilles resilience. Cue: Full range; pause at the top._
- Bent-knee (seated) soleus raise: 1 × 8 @ RIR 4, rest 60 s · _Soleus takes the highest loads in running. Cue: Knee bent ~90°, heavy load, slow._
- Pallof press (per side): 1 × 8 @ RIR 4, rest 60 s · _Anti-rotation trunk control for run stability and aero bars. Cue: Press out, hold 2 s, resist rotation._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* Keep load at ~90% of last week; halve the sets. Leave feeling fresh.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).
*Cues:* Quality over grinding: never to failure; Move the weight with intent on the way up; control down; Lift after the endurance session (≥3–6 h later if possible) to protect key endurance quality
*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

### Wednesday, Jan 20, 2027

**Objective:** Measure Critical Swim Speed to personalise every swim pace.

#### CSS test (400 + 200 time trials) — 2000
Benchmark test · Key — mandatory · 41 min · 2000 yd/m · Test

**Why:** CSS = 200 / (T400 − T200) per unit of distance. It anchors swim zones and the swim-split prediction. Retesting shows whether technique and fitness are translating into speed.

*Warm-up*
- 400 easy — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 drill/swim — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 build to fast — 20 s rest — RPE 5–8/10
- 100 easy — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Main set*
- 400 time trial — push start, maximal even effort — RPE 9/10 · _Record time. Pace evenly: second 200 within ~3 s of the first._
- 200 very easy + 5–8 min rest (full recovery) — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 200 time trial — maximal effort — RPE 9–10/10 · _Record time. Enter both times on the Benchmarks page (pool unit matters)._
*Cool-down*
- 300 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Same pool & unit every retest; Push starts, no dives
*Success:* Both times recorded; T400 > 2 × T200 is normal; if not, the 200 was under-paced
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Psalm 77:19** (Faith in uncertainty) — God's path led through the sea, his way through mighty waters, though his footprints were not seen.
>
> Progress, like God's path, is not always visible. Today's CSS test may show it — or not.
>
> _Trust progress you cannot yet see._

### Thursday, Jan 21, 2027

**Objective:** Establish threshold pace and run LTHR to personalise every run target.

#### 30-minute run threshold test — 60 min
Benchmark test · Key — mandatory · 1 h · Test

**Why:** Friel's 30-minute solo time trial estimates threshold pace (average pace) and LTHR (average HR of the final 20 min). These anchor run zones and the run-split prediction.

*Warm-up*
- 15 min easy — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 20 s strides / 60–90 s walk-jog — RPE 6–7/10 · _Fast and relaxed — about 5K effort, not a sprint. Tall posture, quick light feet._
- 3 min easy — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Main set*
- 30 min solo time trial — as fast as you can evenly sustain — RPE 9/10 · _Press LAP at 10 min. Record total distance, average pace, and average HR for the final 20 min. Most athletes start too fast — the first 5 min should feel controlled._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy jog — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat, uninterrupted route or 400 m track. Same route each retest. No treadmill (pace calibration varies). GPS watch + chest HR strap.
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Rested: no hard session in the prior 48 h; Even pacing — think "negative split"
*Success:* Last 10 min ≥ first 10 min pace; Entered on the Benchmarks page
*Modifications:* fatigue: Reschedule 1–3 days — a tired test produces zones that are too slow for weeks. | weather: Heat >80 °F or strong wind distorts pace — move to early morning or a track; record conditions. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength B — squat + upper push + single-leg — Deload
Strength · Supporting — mandatory · 32 min · RIR 4 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021).

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Back squat or goblet/safety-bar squat: 2 × 5 @ RIR 4, rest 2 min · _Quad & glute strength for bike force and descents. Cue: Controlled depth you own, brace._
- Dumbbell bench press or push-up variation: 2 × 5 @ RIR 4, rest 2 min · _Upper-body maintenance; shoulder balance for swimming. Cue: Shoulder blades set, 2 s lower._
- Single-leg RDL (per leg): 1 × 8 @ RIR 4, rest 60 s · _Hamstring/glute strength & balance for gait. Cue: Hips square; reach long._
- One-arm dumbbell row (per side): 1 × 8 @ RIR 4, rest 60 s · _Pulling strength & posture. Cue: Pull to the hip; no torso twist._
- Step-up to knee drive (per leg): 1 × 8 @ RIR 4, rest 60 s · _Running-specific single-leg power. Cue: Drive through the whole foot._
- Copenhagen side plank (per side, seconds): 1 × 20–30 s @ RIR 4, rest 60 s · _Adductor strength — groin resilience (soccer history). Cue: Start short-lever (knee) and progress._
- Dead bug: 1 × 8 @ RIR 4, rest 60 s · _Deep trunk control. Cue: Low back stays down._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* Keep load at ~90% of last week; halve the sets. Leave feeling fresh.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).
*Cues:* Quality over grinding: never to failure; Move the weight with intent on the way up; control down; Light — after the run test, 2 sets only.
*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Jeremiah 12:5** (Honesty) — God asks Jeremiah: if you have raced with men on foot and they have worn you out, how can you compete with horses?
>
> An honest question for a run test: where are you, really? Honest assessment is the beginning of growth.
>
> _Accept the result as information._

### Friday, Jan 22, 2027

**Objective:** Active recovery and feel for the water.

#### Easy recovery swim — 1400
Recovery swim · Optional · 29 min · 1400 yd/m · Easy

**Why:** Low-stress swimming aids recovery and keeps the stroke fresh.

*Warm-up*
- 300 easy — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Main set*
- 9 × 100 easy, alternating free / choice / pull — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Long, relaxed strokes
*Success:* Felt better leaving than arriving
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

### Saturday, Jan 23, 2027

**Objective:** Establish a validated FTP so every power target in the plan becomes personal.

#### 20-minute FTP test — 65 min
Benchmark test · Key — mandatory · 1 h 05 min · Test

**Why:** Without a measured FTP all bike targets are provisional. FTP anchors training zones, race power, and the bike-split prediction. Retesting every 4–8 weeks shows whether the bike plan is working.

*Warm-up*
- 15 min progressive endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
- 3 × 1 min fast cadence / 1 min easy — RPE 4–5/10
- 5 min at threshold effort (RPE 7) to open up — RPE 7/10
- 10 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Main set*
- 20 min maximal sustainable effort — even pacing — RPE 9/10 · _Start at what you believe is sustainable (RPE ~8) for 5 min, settle, and only push in the final 5 min. Record average power, average HR for the full 20 min, and cadence._
*Cool-down*
- 12 min easy spin, cadence 90+ — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Trainer (ERG off) or a safe, flat, uninterrupted road — use the same setup for every retest. Power meter or smart trainer required for FTP. Without power: record average HR of the 20 min (approximates bike LTHR) and use HR + RPE targets.
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Well rested: no hard training in the prior 48 h; Same time of day, bike, and trainer as future retests; Fan on high
*Success:* Even or slightly negative split (last 5 min ≥ first 5 min); Data entered on the Benchmarks page — FTP ≈ 95% of 20-min average power
*Modifications:* fatigue: If unusually tired, move the test 1–2 days later — a test on tired legs produces wrong zones for 4+ weeks. | weather: Indoors. | time: Do not shorten the warm-up; reschedule instead. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Brick run — 10 min
Brick run · Supporting — mandatory · 10 min · Z2

**Why:** Running economy is impaired immediately after cycling (Millet & Vleck 2000). Regular brick runs train the cycle-to-run transition and teach you to judge race pace when your legs lie to you.

*Warm-up*
- T2 practice: rack bike, helmet off, shoes on, go — target <2 min — RPE 3/10
*Main set*
- 10 min steady aerobic off the bike — 13% slower to 22% slower than threshold pace · HR 85–89% run LTHR · RPE 3–4/10 · _Focus on cadence (≈170–180 spm) in the first 5 min._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min walk — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat loop starting where you finish the ride. Race shoes with elastic laces, race belt, visor, gel.
*Fueling:* Take a gel or 20–25 g carbohydrate in the first 10 min if the run is ≥30 min (practice race-day timing). Drink at a "simulated aid station" every ~1 mile/10 min.
*Cues:* Short quick steps for the first 5 min; Pace by HR/RPE first, then the watch; Note how race pace feels — this calibrates race day
*Success:* Hit pace without exceeding the HR ceiling; Transition <2 min; Log RPE and how legs felt at 5 min vs. 15 min
*Modifications:* fatigue: Run 10 min easy only, or walk 10 min. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: 10 min minimum — the transition itself is the stimulus. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **1 Samuel 16:7** (Identity) — People look at outward appearance, but the Lord looks at the heart.
>
> Today's FTP number is outward; God sees the heart. You are not your watts.
>
> _What does God see in my heart today?_

### Sunday, Jan 24, 2027

**Objective:** Extend running durability so pace holds in the final 5K of the race.

#### Long run — 70 min
Long run · Supporting — mandatory · 1 h 10 min · Z2 aerobic

**Why:** The 70.3 run is decided by durability: holding form and pace after 3+ hours of racing. Long runs build musculoskeletal resilience and fat oxidation; race-pace segments late in the run rehearse running well while fatigued.

*Warm-up*
- 5 min walk → easy jog — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Main set*
- 65 min easy aerobic — 13% slower to 22% slower than threshold pace · HR 85–89% run LTHR · RPE 3–4/10 · _First 10 min easier than you think — practice the patience you need on race day._
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat-to-gently-rolling, ideally crushed gravel/trail (race surface). Loop past your car or a water fountain for fluids. Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals). Handheld/belt with fluids and gels for runs >75 min.
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Run the hills by effort, not pace; Gel practice at the times you will take them in the race; Check form every 10 min: tall, relaxed, quick
*Success:* Pace in the final 20 min ≥ the first 20 min at similar effort; HR drift <5% in the easy portion (where pace is steady); No pain that alters gait
*Modifications:* fatigue: Run 60–70% of the duration, all easy, no race-pace blocks. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep at least 75% of the duration; race-pace blocks stay. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

>

## Week 16 — Race-specific build — week 1 (Mon Jan 25 – Sun Jan 31)

**Phase:** Race-specific build · **Type:** build · **Planned:** 11 h 17 min

Rolling-terrain over-unders; 80 min race power with full race fueling. Strength: one heavy + one short session.

### Monday, Jan 25, 2027

**Objective:** Rest and recover — absorb the training.

#### Rest day
Rest · Key — mandatory · — · Rest

**Why:** Planned rest prevents the slow slide into fatigue that ruins key sessions. Sleep 7–9 h, eat normally (do not "save calories" on rest days), walk if you like.

*Main set*
- No structured training. Optional: 20–30 min easy walk outdoors. — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* — —
*Fueling:* Carbohydrate ≈3–5 g/kg (250–415 g at 83 kg); protein ≈1.6–2.0 g/kg (135–165 g) spread over 4–5 meals. Hydrate normally; pale-yellow urine is a reasonable everyday check.
*Cues:* Bedtime 30 min earlier than usual; Fill in the daily check-in (sleep, fatigue, soreness)
*Success:* You actually rested
*Modifications:* illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Mobility & tissue care — 20 min
Mobility · Optional · 20 min · Recovery

**Why:** Hip-flexor, thoracic, and ankle mobility support an aero position you can hold and a relaxed stride. Low cost, high return on a rest day.

*Main set*
- 90/90 hip switches — 2 × 8 per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Half-kneeling hip-flexor stretch with reach — 2 × 45 s per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Thoracic open-books — 2 × 8 per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Knee-to-wall ankle mobilisation — 2 × 10 per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Band shoulder dislocates + wall slides — 2 × 10 — RPE 1–2/10
- Calf/soleus stretch (straight and bent knee) — 2 × 45 s per side — RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Home Mat, band, optional foam roller.
*Fueling:* Rest days still need carbohydrate (≈3–5 g/kg) and protein (≈1.6–2.0 g/kg) — recovery happens today.
*Cues:* Slow breathing; no pain
*Success:* Done, and you feel looser
*Modifications:* fatigue: Optional — skip if you need sleep more. | time: 10 min version: first three exercises. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Mark 1:35** (Rhythm) — Very early in the morning, while it was still dark, Jesus went to a solitary place and prayed.
>
> Race-specific weeks need rhythm: early mornings, quiet prayer, planned work. Begin the block the way Jesus began his days.
>

### Tuesday, Jan 26, 2027

**Objective:** Teach controlled power on rolling terrain: brief rises above race power without blowing up.

#### Rolling-terrain over-unders 3 × 12 min — 75 min
Race-specific over-unders · Key — mandatory · 1 h 15 min · Race power ↔ threshold

**Why:** The Little Elm course rewards steady power on gradual inclines and descents. Over-unders rehearse the exact skill you missed at Waco: going slightly harder on rises (≤100% FTP, briefly), then recovering at race power rather than coasting or surging.

*Warm-up*
- 10 min progressive spin — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _Start at RPE 2 and finish at the top of endurance._
- 3 × 1 min high-cadence spin-ups (100–110 rpm) / 1 min easy — RPE 3–4/10 · _Smooth pedal stroke; no bouncing in the saddle._
- 3 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Main set*
- 3 × 12 min continuous: alternate 2 min @ 95–100% FTP ("rise") / 2 min @ 78–82% FTP ("roll") — 78–100% FTP · Cadence Rises 75–85 rpm, rolls 85–95 rpm · RPE 6–8/10 · recovery: 5 min easy · _Never above 100% FTP. Shift early before each rise._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy spin, cadence 90+ — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Trainer, or a road with 1–3 min rises. Smart trainer or quiet road loop; power meter if available, HR strap, two bottles, fan indoors.
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Shift before the rise, not halfway up; Over the top: keep pedaling into the descent
*Success:* No spikes above 105% FTP; "Roll" segments stayed at race power, not below 70%
*Modifications:* fatigue: If sleep <6 h, fatigue ≥7/10 or the first rep is a struggle at the low end of the target: drop one rep per set and cap effort at the bottom of the range. If two reps in a row miss by >5%, stop and ride Z2 — that is data, not failure. | weather: Do it indoors. Outdoor wind makes steady power hard; use a flat, safe loop if you must ride outside. | time: Keep warm-up to 12 min and complete two-thirds of the work; do not rush the recoveries. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength A — lower-body pull + upper pull — Race-specific maintenance
Strength · Supporting — mandatory · 39 min · RIR 3 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021).

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Trap-bar deadlift (or barbell RDL if no trap bar): 2 × 3–5 @ RIR 3, rest 2–3 min · _Posterior-chain force for climbing and run propulsion. Cue: Brace, push the floor away, neutral spine._
- Rear-foot-elevated split squat (per leg): 2 × 3–5 @ RIR 3, rest 2–3 min · _Single-leg strength and hip stability for running. Cue: Front shin near vertical, control the descent 2–3 s._
- Pull-up / chin-up (assisted if needed): 2 × 6–8 @ RIR 3, rest 90 s · _Upper-back strength for swim catch and aero posture. Cue: Full hang to chin over bar; ribs down._
- Standing calf raise — slow (3 s down): 2 × 6–8 @ RIR 3, rest 90 s · _Gastrocnemius strength/Achilles resilience. Cue: Full range; pause at the top._
- Bent-knee (seated) soleus raise: 2 × 6–8 @ RIR 3, rest 90 s · _Soleus takes the highest loads in running. Cue: Knee bent ~90°, heavy load, slow._
- Pallof press (per side): 2 × 6–8 @ RIR 3, rest 90 s · _Anti-rotation trunk control for run stability and aero bars. Cue: Press out, hold 2 s, resist rotation._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* Same heavy loads, fewer sets. Never to failure.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).
*Cues:* Quality over grinding: never to failure; Move the weight with intent on the way up; control down; Lift after the endurance session (≥3–6 h later if possible) to protect key endurance quality
*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Proverbs 4:11–12** (Wisdom) — I guide you in the way of wisdom; when you walk, your steps will not be hampered, and when you run, you will not stumble.
>
> Over-unders teach wise pacing on rolling terrain — steps unhampered because they are controlled.
>
> _Shift early on every "rise."_

### Wednesday, Jan 27, 2027

**Objective:** Build the ability to swim the full 1.9 km continuously at controlled effort.

#### Endurance 2 × 800 — 2600
Aerobic endurance · Key — mandatory · 53 min · 2600 yd/m · Aerobic endurance

**Why:** You cannot predict a swim split until you can swim the distance comfortably. Longer repeats at aerobic pace build specific endurance and confidence.

*Warm-up*
- 400 easy freestyle, breathing every 3 strokes — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 drill/swim by 25 (catch-up → fingertip drag) — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Long body line; exhale fully underwater._
*Main set*
- 2 × 800 @ CSS +4 to +6 s/100 — 60 s rest; sight every 6–8 strokes on the final 100 of each — CSS +4 to +6 s/100 · RPE 4–6/10 · _Bilateral breathing; sight with "crocodile eyes" then rotate to breathe._
- 4 × 50 descend 1→4 — 15 s rest — RPE 4–8/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool (50 m/long-course if available) or supervised open water — see safety notes. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Settle into rhythm within 100; Long exhale; relaxed kick (2-beat is fine in a wetsuit)
*Success:* Pace stable across repeats; Comfortable enough to continue
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Easy aerobic run — 40 min
Easy run · Supporting — mandatory · 40 min · Z1–Z2

**Why:** Easy running volume strengthens tendons, bones, and aerobic economy — the foundation for running well off the bike. Keeping easy runs truly easy preserves energy for the sessions that matter (Seiler 2010).

*Main set*
- 40 min easy continuous — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10 · _Easy means you could hold a full conversation. Walk breaks on hills are fine if HR rises above target._
*Terrain/equipment:* Soft or mixed surfaces; include crushed-gravel trail when possible (race surface). Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required.
*Cues:* Cadence quick and light (no overstriding); Relaxed hands and jaw; Nose-breathing test: if you cannot, slow down
*Success:* HR stayed under the Z2 ceiling (or RPE ≤3); Finished feeling you could do more
*Modifications:* fatigue: Replace with 30 min walk or bike spin; easy runs are the first sessions to cut when tired. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Psalm 65:5–7** (Trust) — God is the hope of the ends of the earth and of the farthest seas; he stills the roaring of the seas and their waves.
>
> Two 800s: long enough to need calm. God stills the roaring — inside and out.

### Thursday, Jan 28, 2027

**Objective:** Raise lactate threshold / critical speed — the ceiling under your 70.3 run pace.

#### Threshold cruise intervals 4 × 8 min — 65 min
Threshold intervals · Key — mandatory · 1 h 05 min · Z4 threshold

**Why:** Threshold work raises the speed you can sustain before fatigue accelerates (critical speed; Jones & Vanhatalo 2017). Your off-the-bike half-marathon pace is a fraction of it, so a higher ceiling makes race pace easier.

*Warm-up*
- 10 min easy jog — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
- Drills: 2 × 20 m each — A-skips, high knees, butt kicks, carioca — RPE 3–4/10
- 4 × 20 s strides / 60–90 s walk-jog — RPE 6–7/10 · _Fast and relaxed — about 5K effort, not a sprint. Tall posture, quick light feet._
*Main set*
- 4 × 8 min at threshold — 1% faster to 3% slower than threshold pace · HR 95–99% run LTHR · RPE 7–8/10 · recovery: 120 s easy jog · _"Comfortably hard": controlled, rhythmic breathing; the last rep should be the best one._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy jog + 5 min walk — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat loop, track, or crushed-gravel trail; treadmill at 1% acceptable. Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Even splits — the first rep is where pacing discipline is built; Relax shoulders on every lap
*Success:* All reps within the pace/HR range; Last rep ≤3 s/mi slower than the first
*Modifications:* fatigue: If fatigue ≥7/10, soreness ≥6/10 or sleep <6 h: run easy for 60–70% of the time, no intervals. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength B — squat + upper push + single-leg — Race-specific maintenance
Strength · Supporting — mandatory · 29 min · RIR 3 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021).

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Dumbbell bench press or push-up variation: 2 × 3–5 @ RIR 3, rest 2–3 min · _Upper-body maintenance; shoulder balance for swimming. Cue: Shoulder blades set, 2 s lower._
- Step-up to knee drive (per leg): 2 × 6–8 @ RIR 3, rest 90 s · _Running-specific single-leg power. Cue: Drive through the whole foot._
- Copenhagen side plank (per side, seconds): 2 × 20–30 s @ RIR 3, rest 90 s · _Adductor strength — groin resilience (soccer history). Cue: Start short-lever (knee) and progress._
- Bent-knee (seated) soleus raise: 2 × 6–8 @ RIR 3, rest 90 s · _Soleus takes the highest loads in running. Cue: Knee bent ~90°, heavy load, slow._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* Same heavy loads, fewer sets. Never to failure.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).
*Cues:* Quality over grinding: never to failure; Move the weight with intent on the way up; control down; Lift after the endurance session (≥3–6 h later if possible) to protect key endurance quality
*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Daniel 1:8–16** (Stewardship) — Daniel resolves to keep a disciplined diet, asks for a ten-day trial, and he and his friends end up healthier than the others.
>
> Daniel tested his diet carefully and kept what worked. Fueling is the same: practise, measure, adjust.
>
> _Log what you eat around today's run._

### Friday, Jan 29, 2027

**Objective:** Make open-water navigation, starts, drafting, and turns automatic.

#### Open-water skills (pool) — 2500
Open-water skills · Supporting — mandatory · 51 min · 2500 yd/m · Race effort + skills

**Why:** Poor sighting adds distance; a panicked start wrecks the first 300. These skills are rehearsable in a pool and directly reduce your swim time and stress.

*Warm-up*
- 400 easy freestyle, breathing every 3 strokes — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 drill/swim by 25 (catch-up → fingertip drag) — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Long body line; exhale fully underwater._
*Main set*
- 4 × 100 sighting: lift eyes forward every 6 strokes, then every 4 — 15 s rest — CSS +5 to +9 s/100 · RPE 4–5/10 · _Pick a target on the far wall; eyes only just above water, then roll to breathe._
- 6 × 50 "race start": first 15 strokes fast, then settle to race effort — 20 s rest — RPE 5–8/10 · _Rehearse settling breathing after the surge — the critical skill for a calm start._
- 4 × 100 drafting: swim on a partner's feet or hip (or sight on the lane line) — 15 s rest — CSS +3 to +6 s/100 · RPE 5–6/10 · _Legal in age-group swim. Without a partner: practise swimming straight with eyes closed for 6 strokes, then sight._
- 2 × 300 continuous at race effort with mid-pool "buoy turn" (no wall push) each 100 — 30 s rest — CSS +3 to +6 s/100 · RPE 5–6/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool (50 m/long-course if available) or supervised open water — see safety notes. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Sight → breathe in one fluid motion; Turn at "buoys" by rolling onto your back briefly or a tight corkscrew
*Success:* Sighting without stopping the stroke; Calm breathing within 30 s of each start surge
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Acts 27:21–25** (Courage) — In a violent storm at sea, Paul tells the crew to take courage, because God has assured him they will survive.
>
> The 2026 race swim was shortened by wind. Whatever the lake brings, you can take courage.
>
> _Practise a calm start in every 50._

### Saturday, Jan 30, 2027

**Objective:** Extend aerobic durability and practice race nutrition.

#### Long ride + race-specific blocks — 3:15 h
Long ride + race-specific blocks · Key — mandatory · 3 h 15 min · Z2 with race-power / tempo blocks

**Why:** Long rides develop the fatigue resistance that decides the second half of the bike and the whole run. Race-power blocks late in the ride teach you what 75–80% FTP feels like when you are already tired.

*Warm-up*
- 20 min progressive from easy to endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
*Main set*
- 30 min endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _First fuel at 20 min. Settle in; this is where Waco-style over-pacing starts if you let it._
- 2 × 40 min race power — full race fueling (80–90 g/h) — 75–80% FTP · HR 86–92% bike LTHR · Cadence 82–92 rpm · RPE 5–6/10 · recovery: 10 min endurance between
- 45 min endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _Final 10 min: shift to an easier gear, spin 90+ rpm, finish the bottle — prepare legs for the brick._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Rolling route similar to the race (gradual rises, long open roads); stay in aero on safe straights. Trainer if weather or safety requires. Race bike in race position, power meter/HR, race nutrition, saddle bag with tube/CO₂/levers, phone, ID.
*Fueling:* Before: 1–2 g/kg carbohydrate (80–160 g) 2–4 h prior — rehearse your race breakfast. During: 60–90 g/h carbohydrate from glucose + fructose sources (build toward 80–90 g/h by the race-specific phase); 500–800 mL/h fluid adjusted to sweat rate; ~300–600 mg sodium per liter (more if you are a heavy/salty sweater). Log grams per hour and any GI symptoms — this is gut training. Keep eating in the final 20 min of the ride — fuel for the run starts on the bike. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Eat every 20 min, drink every 10–15 min; In aero: chin down, shoulders relaxed, elbows narrow; On rises: shift early, hold power, do not chase speed; On descents: keep light pressure, do not coast if speed is low
*Success:* Normalized power ≤ 1.05 × average power (Variability Index ≤1.05) on steady segments; Hit fueling target (log grams/hour); Blocks within target; final hour not slower than the first
*Modifications:* fatigue: Remove the blocks and ride 70–80% of the duration in Z2. If fatigue is high for 2+ days, ride 60–75 min easy instead. | weather: Rain, ice, lightning, or wind gusts >25 mph: ride indoors at 80–90% duration (indoor riding is harder per minute — continuous pedaling, more heat). | time: Minimum 75% of duration with all blocks intact; trim endurance riding first. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Brick run — 30 min (20 min race pace)
Brick run · Key — mandatory · 30 min · 70.3 race pace

**Why:** Running economy is impaired immediately after cycling (Millet & Vleck 2000). Regular brick runs train the cycle-to-run transition and teach you to judge race pace when your legs lie to you.

*Warm-up*
- T2 practice: rack bike, helmet off, shoes on, go — target <2 min — RPE 3/10
*Main set*
- First 20 min at 70.3 race pace — 6% slower to 11% slower than threshold pace · HR 89–93% run LTHR · RPE 5–6/10 · _First 3–5 min: quick, short steps; let the legs come around. Do not chase pace in mile 1 — that is the Waco lesson._
- 10 min easy — 13% slower to 22% slower than threshold pace · HR 85–89% run LTHR · RPE 3–4/10
*Cool-down*
- 5 min walk — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat loop starting where you finish the ride. Race shoes with elastic laces, race belt, visor, gel.
*Fueling:* Take a gel or 20–25 g carbohydrate in the first 10 min if the run is ≥30 min (practice race-day timing). Drink at a "simulated aid station" every ~1 mile/10 min.
*Cues:* Short quick steps for the first 5 min; Pace by HR/RPE first, then the watch; Note how race pace feels — this calibrates race day
*Success:* Hit pace without exceeding the HR ceiling; Transition <2 min; Log RPE and how legs felt at 5 min vs. 15 min
*Modifications:* fatigue: Run 10 min easy only, or walk 10 min. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: 10 min minimum — the transition itself is the stimulus. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **1 Corinthians 10:31** (Stewardship) — Whether you eat or drink, or whatever you do, do it all for the glory of God.
>
> Full race fueling today: 80–90 g/h. Even eating and drinking can be done to God's glory.
>
> _Hit your fueling plan as an act of stewardship._

### Sunday, Jan 31, 2027

**Objective:** Extend running durability so pace holds in the final 5K of the race.

#### Long run — 100 min with race-pace work
Long run · Key — mandatory · 1 h 40 min · Z2 + 70.3 race pace

**Why:** The 70.3 run is decided by durability: holding form and pace after 3+ hours of racing. Long runs build musculoskeletal resilience and fat oxidation; race-pace segments late in the run rehearse running well while fatigued.

*Warm-up*
- 5 min walk → easy jog — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Main set*
- 26 min easy aerobic — 13% slower to 22% slower than threshold pace · HR 85–89% run LTHR · RPE 3–4/10 · _First 10 min easier than you think — practice the patience you need on race day._
- 3 × 15 min at 70.3 race pace — 6% slower to 11% slower than threshold pace · HR 89–93% run LTHR · RPE 5–6/10 · recovery: 5 min easy jog
- 14 min easy — 13% slower to 22% slower than threshold pace · HR 85–89% run LTHR · RPE 3–4/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat-to-gently-rolling, ideally crushed gravel/trail (race surface). Loop past your car or a water fountain for fluids. Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals). Handheld/belt with fluids and gels for runs >75 min.
*Fueling:* Before: 1–2 g/kg carbohydrate (80–160 g) 2–3 h prior; familiar, low-fiber foods. During: 45–60 g/h carbohydrate starting at 20 min; 500–750 mL/h fluid adjusted to sweat rate and weather; ~300–600 mg sodium per liter. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Run the hills by effort, not pace; Gel practice at the times you will take them in the race; Check form every 10 min: tall, relaxed, quick
*Success:* Pace in the final 20 min ≥ the first 20 min at similar effort; HR drift <5% in the easy portion (where pace is steady); No pain that alters gait
*Modifications:* fatigue: Run 60–70% of the duration, all easy, no race-pace blocks. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep at least 75% of the duration; race-pace blocks stay. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Hebrews 12:12–13** (Perseverance) — Strengthen your feeble arms and weak knees; make level paths for your feet so the lame may be healed.
>
> Race-pace segments on tired legs: strengthen the knees, level the path.
>
> _Hold form in the last race-pace block._

## Week 17 — Race-specific build — week 2 (Mon Feb 1 – Sun Feb 7)

**Phase:** Race-specific build · **Type:** build · **Planned:** 12 h 04 min

Longest long run (105 min). 90 min race power.

### Monday, Feb 1, 2027

**Objective:** Rest and recover — absorb the training.

#### Rest day
Rest · Key — mandatory · — · Rest

**Why:** Planned rest prevents the slow slide into fatigue that ruins key sessions. Sleep 7–9 h, eat normally (do not "save calories" on rest days), walk if you like.

*Main set*
- No structured training. Optional: 20–30 min easy walk outdoors. — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* — —
*Fueling:* Carbohydrate ≈3–5 g/kg (250–415 g at 83 kg); protein ≈1.6–2.0 g/kg (135–165 g) spread over 4–5 meals. Hydrate normally; pale-yellow urine is a reasonable everyday check.
*Cues:* Bedtime 30 min earlier than usual; Fill in the daily check-in (sleep, fatigue, soreness)
*Success:* You actually rested
*Modifications:* illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Mobility & tissue care — 20 min
Mobility · Optional · 20 min · Recovery

**Why:** Hip-flexor, thoracic, and ankle mobility support an aero position you can hold and a relaxed stride. Low cost, high return on a rest day.

*Main set*
- 90/90 hip switches — 2 × 8 per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Half-kneeling hip-flexor stretch with reach — 2 × 45 s per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Thoracic open-books — 2 × 8 per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Knee-to-wall ankle mobilisation — 2 × 10 per side — RPE 1–2/10
- Band shoulder dislocates + wall slides — 2 × 10 — RPE 1–2/10
- Calf/soleus stretch (straight and bent knee) — 2 × 45 s per side — RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Home Mat, band, optional foam roller.
*Fueling:* Rest days still need carbohydrate (≈3–5 g/kg) and protein (≈1.6–2.0 g/kg) — recovery happens today.
*Cues:* Slow breathing; no pain
*Success:* Done, and you feel looser
*Modifications:* fatigue: Optional — skip if you need sleep more. | time: 10 min version: first three exercises. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Psalm 3:5** (Rest) — I lie down and sleep; I wake again, because the Lord sustains me.
>
> The highest-volume week of the plan. Sleep is sustained by the Lord — receive it.
>
> _Get to bed early every night this week._

### Tuesday, Feb 2, 2027

**Objective:** Teach controlled power on rolling terrain: brief rises above race power without blowing up.

#### Rolling-terrain over-unders 3 × 15 min — 84 min
Race-specific over-unders · Key — mandatory · 1 h 24 min · Race power ↔ threshold

**Why:** The Little Elm course rewards steady power on gradual inclines and descents. Over-unders rehearse the exact skill you missed at Waco: going slightly harder on rises (≤100% FTP, briefly), then recovering at race power rather than coasting or surging.

*Warm-up*
- 10 min progressive spin — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _Start at RPE 2 and finish at the top of endurance._
- 3 × 1 min high-cadence spin-ups (100–110 rpm) / 1 min easy — RPE 3–4/10 · _Smooth pedal stroke; no bouncing in the saddle._
- 3 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Main set*
- 3 × 15 min continuous: alternate 2 min @ 95–100% FTP ("rise") / 2 min @ 78–82% FTP ("roll") — 78–100% FTP · Cadence Rises 75–85 rpm, rolls 85–95 rpm · RPE 6–8/10 · recovery: 5 min easy · _Never above 100% FTP. Shift early before each rise._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy spin, cadence 90+ — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Trainer, or a road with 1–3 min rises. Smart trainer or quiet road loop; power meter if available, HR strap, two bottles, fan indoors.
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Shift before the rise, not halfway up; Over the top: keep pedaling into the descent
*Success:* No spikes above 105% FTP; "Roll" segments stayed at race power, not below 70%
*Modifications:* fatigue: If sleep <6 h, fatigue ≥7/10 or the first rep is a struggle at the low end of the target: drop one rep per set and cap effort at the bottom of the range. If two reps in a row miss by >5%, stop and ride Z2 — that is data, not failure. | weather: Do it indoors. Outdoor wind makes steady power hard; use a flat, safe loop if you must ride outside. | time: Keep warm-up to 12 min and complete two-thirds of the work; do not rush the recoveries. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength A — lower-body pull + upper pull — Race-specific maintenance
Strength · Supporting — mandatory · 39 min · RIR 3 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021).

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Trap-bar deadlift (or barbell RDL if no trap bar): 2 × 3–5 @ RIR 3, rest 2–3 min · _Posterior-chain force for climbing and run propulsion. Cue: Brace, push the floor away, neutral spine._
- Rear-foot-elevated split squat (per leg): 2 × 3–5 @ RIR 3, rest 2–3 min · _Single-leg strength and hip stability for running. Cue: Front shin near vertical, control the descent 2–3 s._
- Pull-up / chin-up (assisted if needed): 2 × 6–8 @ RIR 3, rest 90 s · _Upper-back strength for swim catch and aero posture. Cue: Full hang to chin over bar; ribs down._
- Standing calf raise — slow (3 s down): 2 × 6–8 @ RIR 3, rest 90 s · _Gastrocnemius strength/Achilles resilience. Cue: Full range; pause at the top._
- Bent-knee (seated) soleus raise: 2 × 6–8 @ RIR 3, rest 90 s · _Soleus takes the highest loads in running. Cue: Knee bent ~90°, heavy load, slow._
- Pallof press (per side): 2 × 6–8 @ RIR 3, rest 90 s · _Anti-rotation trunk control for run stability and aero bars. Cue: Press out, hold 2 s, resist rotation._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* Same heavy loads, fewer sets. Never to failure.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).
*Cues:* Quality over grinding: never to failure; Move the weight with intent on the way up; control down; Lift after the endurance session (≥3–6 h later if possible) to protect key endurance quality
*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Philippians 2:3–4** (Humility) — Do nothing from selfish ambition or vain conceit; in humility value others above yourselves.
>
> Training can turn self-focused. Today, look up from your power numbers to see the people around you.
