### Saturday, Feb 27, 2027

**Objective:** Rehearse race pacing, position, fueling, and the transition to running.

#### Race simulation — 2:45 h
Race simulation · Key — mandatory · 2 h 45 min · Z2 with race-power / tempo blocks

**Why:** Long rides develop the fatigue resistance that decides the second half of the bike and the whole run. Race-power blocks late in the ride teach you what 75–80% FTP feels like when you are already tired.

*Warm-up*
- 20 min progressive from easy to endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
*Main set*
- 18 min endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _First fuel at 20 min. Settle in; this is where Waco-style over-pacing starts if you let it._
- 1 × 90 min race power — race kit, race nutrition, race position — 75–80% FTP · HR 86–92% bike LTHR · Cadence 82–92 rpm · RPE 5–6/10
- 27 min endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _Final 10 min: shift to an easier gear, spin 90+ rpm, finish the bottle — prepare legs for the brick._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Rolling route similar to the race (gradual rises, long open roads); stay in aero on safe straights. Trainer if weather or safety requires. Race bike in race position, power meter/HR, race nutrition, saddle bag with tube/CO₂/levers, phone, ID.
*Fueling:* Before: 1–2 g/kg carbohydrate (80–160 g) 2–4 h prior — rehearse your race breakfast. During: 60–90 g/h carbohydrate from glucose + fructose sources (build toward 80–90 g/h by the race-specific phase); 500–800 mL/h fluid adjusted to sweat rate; ~300–600 mg sodium per liter (more if you are a heavy/salty sweater). Log grams per hour and any GI symptoms — this is gut training. Keep eating in the final 20 min of the ride — fuel for the run starts on the bike. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Eat every 20 min, drink every 10–15 min; In aero: chin down, shoulders relaxed, elbows narrow; On rises: shift early, hold power, do not chase speed; On descents: keep light pressure, do not coast if speed is low
*Success:* Normalized power ≤ 1.05 × average power (Variability Index ≤1.05) on steady segments; Hit fueling target (log grams/hour); Blocks within target; final hour not slower than the first
*Modifications:* fatigue: Remove the blocks and ride 70–80% of the duration in Z2. If fatigue is high for 2+ days, ride 60–75 min easy instead. | weather: Rain, ice, lightning, or wind gusts >25 mph: ride indoors at 80–90% duration (indoor riding is harder per minute — continuous pedaling, more heat). | time: Minimum 75% of duration with all blocks intact; trim endurance riding first. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Brick run — 45 min (30 min race pace)
Brick run · Key — mandatory · 45 min · 70.3 race pace

**Why:** Running economy is impaired immediately after cycling (Millet & Vleck 2000). Regular brick runs train the cycle-to-run transition and teach you to judge race pace when your legs lie to you.

*Warm-up*
- T2 practice: rack bike, helmet off, shoes on, go — target <2 min — RPE 3/10
*Main set*
- First 30 min at 70.3 race pace — 6% slower to 11% slower than threshold pace · HR 89–93% run LTHR · RPE 5–6/10 · _First 3–5 min: quick, short steps; let the legs come around. Do not chase pace in mile 1 — that is the Waco lesson._
- 15 min easy — 13% slower to 22% slower than threshold pace · HR 85–89% run LTHR · RPE 3–4/10
*Cool-down*
- 5 min walk — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat loop starting where you finish the ride. Race shoes with elastic laces, race belt, visor, gel.
*Fueling:* Take a gel or 20–25 g carbohydrate in the first 10 min if the run is ≥30 min (practice race-day timing). Drink at a "simulated aid station" every ~1 mile/10 min.
*Cues:* Short quick steps for the first 5 min; Pace by HR/RPE first, then the watch; Note how race pace feels — this calibrates race day
*Success:* Hit pace without exceeding the HR ceiling; Transition <2 min; Log RPE and how legs felt at 5 min vs. 15 min
*Modifications:* fatigue: Run 10 min easy only, or walk 10 min. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: 10 min minimum — the transition itself is the stimulus. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **1 Kings 18:41–46** (Strength) — After Elijah prays and the long-awaited rain comes, the power of the Lord comes on him and he runs ahead of Ahab's chariot all the way to Jezreel.
>
> Race simulation: the bike, then the run. Elijah ran after he prayed. Pray, then race your plan.
>
> _Run the brick with the strength God gives._

### Sunday, Feb 28, 2027

**Objective:** Build running durability and aerobic economy at low injury risk.

#### Easy aerobic run — 60 min
Easy run · Supporting — mandatory · 1 h · Z1–Z2

**Why:** Easy running volume strengthens tendons, bones, and aerobic economy — the foundation for running well off the bike. Keeping easy runs truly easy preserves energy for the sessions that matter (Seiler 2010). Recovery long run — easy only.

*Main set*
- 60 min easy continuous — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10 · _Easy means you could hold a full conversation. Walk breaks on hills are fine if HR rises above target._
*Terrain/equipment:* Soft or mixed surfaces; include crushed-gravel trail when possible (race surface). Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: water to thirst; 0–30 g/h carbohydrate is fine for easy aerobic work. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Cadence quick and light (no overstriding); Relaxed hands and jaw; Nose-breathing test: if you cannot, slow down
*Success:* HR stayed under the Z2 ceiling (or RPE ≤3); Finished feeling you could do more
*Modifications:* fatigue: Replace with 30 min walk or bike spin; easy runs are the first sessions to cut when tired. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Mark 2:27** (Rest) — The Sabbath was made for people, not people for the Sabbath.
>
> Rest serves you; you do not serve the plan. Today's run is recovery after the biggest day of the cycle.
>
> _Keep the run truly easy._

## Week 21 — Taper (Mon Mar 1 – Sun Mar 7)

**Phase:** Taper · **Type:** taper · **Planned:** 7 h 42 min

Same rhythm, shorter sessions, intensity kept. Final strength session Tuesday.

**Taper:** volume — ≈34% below the Build 2 average (11.5 h → 7.7 h), then ≈70% below in race week excluding the race itself — a 2-week progressive taper (Bosquet 2007 found 8–14 days with 41–60% volume reduction most effective). Kept — Race-pace bike, threshold run reps, race-pace swimming — intensity and frequency maintained (Mujika & Padilla 2003). Removed — Long-ride duration (3:30 → 2:00); long run (105 → 60 min); second strength session. Lifting — One short session, ~80% loads, ≥10 days before race. Monitor — Expect restless "taper legs." Fatigue should fall; resting HR and mood should improve. Do not add sessions.

### Monday, Mar 1, 2027

**Objective:** Rest and recover — absorb the training.

#### Rest day
Rest · Key — mandatory · — · Rest

**Why:** Planned rest prevents the slow slide into fatigue that ruins key sessions. Sleep 7–9 h, eat normally (do not "save calories" on rest days), walk if you like.

*Main set*
- No structured training. Optional: 20–30 min easy walk outdoors. — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* — —
*Fueling:* Carbohydrate ≈3–5 g/kg (250–415 g at 83 kg); protein ≈1.6–2.0 g/kg (135–165 g) spread over 4–5 meals. Hydrate normally; pale-yellow urine is a reasonable everyday check.
*Cues:* Bedtime 30 min earlier than usual; Fill in the daily check-in (sleep, fatigue, soreness)
*Success:* You actually rested
*Modifications:* illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Isaiah 32:17–18** (Peace) — The fruit of righteousness will be peace; its effect will be quietness and confidence forever; God's people will live in peaceful dwelling places and undisturbed resting places.
>
> Taper week: quietness and confidence. You are not losing fitness; you are revealing it.
>
> _Trust the taper._

### Tuesday, Mar 2, 2027

**Objective:** Maintain sharpness and confirm race power feels controlled while fatigue falls.

#### Race-week opener — 60 min
Opener · Key — mandatory · 1 h · Race power + short openers

**Why:** Tapering research supports cutting volume while keeping some intensity (Bosquet 2007; Mujika & Padilla 2003). Short race-pace efforts keep the engine tuned without adding fatigue.

*Warm-up*
- 15 min progressive — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
*Main set*
- 3 × 5 min at race power — 75–80% FTP · HR 86–92% bike LTHR · Cadence 82–92 rpm · RPE 5–6/10 · recovery: 3 min easy · _Note HR and RPE at race power; it should feel controlled._
- 3 × 30 s at 110% FTP — 108–112% FTP · Cadence 95–105 rpm · RPE 7–8/10 · recovery: 90 s easy
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy spin, cadence 90+ — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Race bike on the road if safe (check shifting, brakes, tire pressure). Race bike exactly as it will be raced.
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Do not test fitness — fitness is banked; Check every piece of equipment
*Success:* Race power felt like RPE 5; Bike mechanically perfect
*Modifications:* fatigue: Ride 30 min easy with 2 × 3 min race power only. | weather: Trainer. | time: Minimum 30 min with the race-power reps. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Strength Full body — Final strength touch
Strength · Optional · 24 min · RIR 4 on main lifts

**Why:** Heavy, low-rep strength work improves cycling and running economy and late-race power without the bulk endurance athletes fear (Rønnestad & Mujika 2014; Beattie 2014). It also protects the strength you value. Volume falls as the race approaches but load is kept, because intensity — not volume — preserves strength (Spiering 2021).

*Warm-up*
- 5 min easy bike/row + 5 min dynamic mobility: leg swings, world's greatest stretch, glute bridges, band pull-aparts — RPE 2–3/10
- Ramp-up sets on the first lift: 2–3 progressively heavier sets of 3–5 — RPE 3–5/10
*Main set*
- Trap-bar deadlift (or barbell RDL if no trap bar): 2 × 3 @ RIR 4, rest 2 min · _Posterior-chain force for climbing and run propulsion. Cue: Brace, push the floor away, neutral spine._
- Back squat or goblet/safety-bar squat: 2 × 3 @ RIR 4, rest 2 min · _Quad & glute strength for bike force and descents. Cue: Controlled depth you own, brace._
- Bent-knee (seated) soleus raise: 1 × 8 @ RIR 4, rest 60 s · _Soleus takes the highest loads in running. Cue: Knee bent ~90°, heavy load, slow._
- Copenhagen side plank (per side, seconds): 1 × 20–30 s @ RIR 4, rest 60 s · _Adductor strength — groin resilience (soccer history). Cue: Start short-lever (knee) and progress._
*Cool-down*
- 5 min: hip-flexor stretch, calf stretch, thoracic rotations — RPE 1–2/10
*Load:* ~80% of block loads; last lifting session ≥10 days before race day.
*Terrain/equipment:* Gym. Trap bar/barbell, dumbbells, bench, pull-up bar, cable or band.
*Fueling:* Have 25–35 g protein within ~2 h after lifting (spread protein across 4–5 meals daily).
*Cues:* Quality over grinding: never to failure; Move the weight with intent on the way up; control down; Lift after the endurance session (≥3–6 h later if possible) to protect key endurance quality
*Success:* All sets at the prescribed RIR; Log load × reps for the main lifts (strength tracking); No soreness that affects the next day's run
*Modifications:* fatigue: Do main lifts only (2 sets) at the same load; skip accessories. Do not drop the load first — drop sets. | time: Main lifts + calf/soleus only (≈25 min). | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **John 15:1–2** (Pruning) — God the gardener prunes every branch that bears fruit so that it will bear even more.
>
> A taper is pruning: removing volume so performance can show. Pruning looks like loss and produces fruit.
>
> _What else in my life needs pruning?_

### Wednesday, Mar 3, 2027

**Objective:** Raise sustainable swim speed (threshold).

#### CSS set 3 × 500 — 2500
Threshold (CSS) · Key — mandatory · 51 min · 2500 yd/m · CSS / threshold

**Why:** CSS work is the most time-efficient way for age-group triathletes to raise sustainable swim pace. A faster CSS means 70.3 race effort (CSS +3–6 s/100) costs less, so you exit the water fresher.

*Warm-up*
- 400 easy freestyle, breathing every 3 strokes — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 drill/swim by 25 (catch-up → fingertip drag) — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Long body line; exhale fully underwater._
*Main set*
- 3 × 500 @ CSS +3 to +5 s/100 — 30 s rest — CSS +3 to +5 s/100 · RPE 6–8/10 · _Even pace. Use the pace clock; stroke count steady._
- 200 pull with buoy, easy-moderate — CSS +5 to +9 s/100 · RPE 4–5/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Hold the same pace on every rep — the last one should not be the slowest; Rotate to breathe, do not lift the head
*Success:* ≥80% of reps within target; Stroke count rose ≤2 per length late in the set
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Easy aerobic run — 30 min
Easy run · Supporting — mandatory · 30 min · Z1–Z2

**Why:** Easy running volume strengthens tendons, bones, and aerobic economy — the foundation for running well off the bike. Keeping easy runs truly easy preserves energy for the sessions that matter (Seiler 2010).

*Main set*
- 30 min easy continuous — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10 · _Easy means you could hold a full conversation. Walk breaks on hills are fine if HR rises above target._
*Terrain/equipment:* Soft or mixed surfaces; include crushed-gravel trail when possible (race surface). Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required.
*Cues:* Cadence quick and light (no overstriding); Relaxed hands and jaw; Nose-breathing test: if you cannot, slow down
*Success:* HR stayed under the Z2 ceiling (or RPE ≤3); Finished feeling you could do more
*Modifications:* fatigue: Replace with 30 min walk or bike spin; easy runs are the first sessions to cut when tired. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Isaiah 55:8–11** (Trust) — God's thoughts are higher than ours; like rain that waters the earth, his word does not return empty but accomplishes its purpose.
>
> The rain has fallen for 21 weeks. It will not return empty.
>

### Thursday, Mar 4, 2027

**Objective:** Raise lactate threshold / critical speed — the ceiling under your 70.3 run pace.

#### Threshold cruise intervals 4 × 5 min — 52 min
Threshold intervals · Key — mandatory · 52 min · Z4 threshold

**Why:** Threshold work raises the speed you can sustain before fatigue accelerates (critical speed; Jones & Vanhatalo 2017). Your off-the-bike half-marathon pace is a fraction of it, so a higher ceiling makes race pace easier.

*Warm-up*
- 10 min easy jog — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
- Drills: 2 × 20 m each — A-skips, high knees, butt kicks, carioca — RPE 3–4/10
- 4 × 20 s strides / 60–90 s walk-jog — RPE 6–7/10 · _Fast and relaxed — about 5K effort, not a sprint. Tall posture, quick light feet._
*Main set*
- 4 × 5 min at threshold — 1% faster to 3% slower than threshold pace · HR 95–99% run LTHR · RPE 7–8/10 · recovery: 90 s easy jog · _"Comfortably hard": controlled, rhythmic breathing; the last rep should be the best one._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy jog + 5 min walk — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat loop, track, or crushed-gravel trail; treadmill at 1% acceptable. Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required. Optional: carbohydrate drink or mouth-rinse if training fasted. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Even splits — the first rep is where pacing discipline is built; Relax shoulders on every lap
*Success:* All reps within the pace/HR range; Last rep ≤3 s/mi slower than the first
*Modifications:* fatigue: If fatigue ≥7/10, soreness ≥6/10 or sleep <6 h: run easy for 60–70% of the time, no intervals. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Philippians 1:6** (Confidence) — He who began a good work in you will carry it to completion.
>
> Your preparation began in October. Today's threshold reps are confirmation, not a final exam.
>
> _Thank God for the work he has begun in you._

### Friday, Mar 5, 2027

**Objective:** Make open-water navigation, starts, drafting, and turns automatic.

#### Open-water skills (pool) — 2200
Open-water skills · Supporting — mandatory · 45 min · 2200 yd/m · Race effort + skills

**Why:** Poor sighting adds distance; a panicked start wrecks the first 300. These skills are rehearsable in a pool and directly reduce your swim time and stress.

*Warm-up*
- 400 easy freestyle, breathing every 3 strokes — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
- 4 × 50 drill/swim by 25 (catch-up → fingertip drag) — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10 · _Long body line; exhale fully underwater._
*Main set*
- 4 × 100 sighting: lift eyes forward every 6 strokes, then every 4 — 15 s rest — CSS +5 to +9 s/100 · RPE 4–5/10 · _Pick a target on the far wall; eyes only just above water, then roll to breathe._
- 6 × 50 "race start": first 15 strokes fast, then settle to race effort — 20 s rest — RPE 5–8/10 · _Rehearse settling breathing after the surge — the critical skill for a calm start._
- 4 × 100 drafting: swim on a partner's feet or hip (or sight on the lane line) — 15 s rest — CSS +3 to +6 s/100 · RPE 5–6/10 · _Legal in age-group swim. Without a partner: practise swimming straight with eyes closed for 6 strokes, then sight._
- 1 × 300 continuous at race effort with mid-pool "buoy turn" (no wall push) each 100 — 30 s rest — CSS +3 to +6 s/100 · RPE 5–6/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool (50 m/long-course if available) or supervised open water — see safety notes. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Sight → breathe in one fluid motion; Turn at "buoys" by rolling onto your back briefly or a tight corkscrew
*Success:* Sighting without stopping the stroke; Calm breathing within 30 s of each start surge
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Psalm 46:1–3** (Refuge) — God is our refuge and strength, an ever-present help in trouble; we will not fear though the waters roar and foam.
>
> Waters roar: race morning might be windy. God is an ever-present help.
>
> _Rehearse your calm start once more._

### Saturday, Mar 6, 2027

**Objective:** Extend aerobic durability and practice race nutrition.

#### Long ride + race-specific blocks — 2 h
Long ride + race-specific blocks · Key — mandatory · 2 h · Z2 with race-power / tempo blocks

**Why:** Long rides develop the fatigue resistance that decides the second half of the bike and the whole run. Race-power blocks late in the ride teach you what 75–80% FTP feels like when you are already tired.

*Warm-up*
- 20 min progressive from easy to endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
*Main set*
- 18 min endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _First fuel at 20 min. Settle in; this is where Waco-style over-pacing starts if you let it._
- 2 × 20 min race power — 75–80% FTP · HR 86–92% bike LTHR · Cadence 82–92 rpm · RPE 5–6/10 · recovery: 5 min endurance between
- 27 min endurance — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10 · _Final 10 min: shift to an easier gear, spin 90+ rpm, finish the bottle — prepare legs for the brick._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Rolling route similar to the race (gradual rises, long open roads); stay in aero on safe straights. Trainer if weather or safety requires. Race bike in race position, power meter/HR, race nutrition, saddle bag with tube/CO₂/levers, phone, ID.
*Fueling:* Before: 1–2 g/kg carbohydrate (80–160 g) 2–3 h prior; familiar, low-fiber foods. During: 45–60 g/h carbohydrate starting at 20 min; 500–750 mL/h fluid adjusted to sweat rate and weather; ~300–600 mg sodium per liter. Keep eating in the final 20 min of the ride — fuel for the run starts on the bike. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Eat every 20 min, drink every 10–15 min; In aero: chin down, shoulders relaxed, elbows narrow; On rises: shift early, hold power, do not chase speed; On descents: keep light pressure, do not coast if speed is low
*Success:* Normalized power ≤ 1.05 × average power (Variability Index ≤1.05) on steady segments; Hit fueling target (log grams/hour); Blocks within target; final hour not slower than the first
*Modifications:* fatigue: Remove the blocks and ride 70–80% of the duration in Z2. If fatigue is high for 2+ days, ride 60–75 min easy instead. | weather: Rain, ice, lightning, or wind gusts >25 mph: ride indoors at 80–90% duration (indoor riding is harder per minute — continuous pedaling, more heat). | time: Minimum 75% of duration with all blocks intact; trim endurance riding first. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Brick run — 20 min (15 min race pace)
Brick run · Key — mandatory · 20 min · 70.3 race pace

**Why:** Running economy is impaired immediately after cycling (Millet & Vleck 2000). Regular brick runs train the cycle-to-run transition and teach you to judge race pace when your legs lie to you.

*Warm-up*
- T2 practice: rack bike, helmet off, shoes on, go — target <2 min — RPE 3/10
*Main set*
- First 15 min at 70.3 race pace — 6% slower to 11% slower than threshold pace · HR 89–93% run LTHR · RPE 5–6/10 · _First 3–5 min: quick, short steps; let the legs come around. Do not chase pace in mile 1 — that is the Waco lesson._
- 5 min easy — 13% slower to 22% slower than threshold pace · HR 85–89% run LTHR · RPE 3–4/10
*Cool-down*
- 5 min walk — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat loop starting where you finish the ride. Race shoes with elastic laces, race belt, visor, gel.
*Fueling:* Take a gel or 20–25 g carbohydrate in the first 10 min if the run is ≥30 min (practice race-day timing). Drink at a "simulated aid station" every ~1 mile/10 min.
*Cues:* Short quick steps for the first 5 min; Pace by HR/RPE first, then the watch; Note how race pace feels — this calibrates race day
*Success:* Hit pace without exceeding the HR ceiling; Transition <2 min; Log RPE and how legs felt at 5 min vs. 15 min
*Modifications:* fatigue: Run 10 min easy only, or walk 10 min. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: 10 min minimum — the transition itself is the stimulus. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

### Sunday, Mar 7, 2027

**Objective:** Extend running durability so pace holds in the final 5K of the race.

#### Long run — 60 min with race-pace work
Long run · Key — mandatory · 1 h · Z2 + 70.3 race pace

**Why:** The 70.3 run is decided by durability: holding form and pace after 3+ hours of racing. Long runs build musculoskeletal resilience and fat oxidation; race-pace segments late in the run rehearse running well while fatigued.

*Warm-up*
- 5 min walk → easy jog — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Main set*
- 18 min easy aerobic — 13% slower to 22% slower than threshold pace · HR 85–89% run LTHR · RPE 3–4/10 · _First 10 min easier than you think — practice the patience you need on race day._
- 3 × 8 min at 70.3 race pace — 6% slower to 11% slower than threshold pace · HR 89–93% run LTHR · RPE 5–6/10 · recovery: 2 min easy jog
- 9 min easy — 13% slower to 22% slower than threshold pace · HR 85–89% run LTHR · RPE 3–4/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat-to-gently-rolling, ideally crushed gravel/trail (race surface). Loop past your car or a water fountain for fluids. Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals). Handheld/belt with fluids and gels for runs >75 min.
*Fueling:* Before: 1 g/kg carbohydrate (~80 g) 1–3 h prior, e.g., oatmeal + banana + honey. During: 30–60 g/h carbohydrate (one 500–750 mL bottle with ~40 g drink mix) — quality intervals deserve carbohydrate. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Run the hills by effort, not pace; Gel practice at the times you will take them in the race; Check form every 10 min: tall, relaxed, quick
*Success:* Pace in the final 20 min ≥ the first 20 min at similar effort; HR drift <5% in the easy portion (where pace is steady); No pain that alters gait
*Modifications:* fatigue: Run 60–70% of the duration, all easy, no race-pace blocks. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep at least 75% of the duration; race-pace blocks stay. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Matthew 25:21** (Faithfulness) — The master says, "Well done, good and faithful servant; you have been faithful with a few things."
>
> Faithfulness, not results, is what the master praises. You have been faithful.
>

## Week 22 — Race week (Mon Mar 8 – Sun Mar 14)

**Phase:** Race week · **Type:** race · **Planned:** 8 h 16 min

Openers, logistics, sleep, carbohydrate loading (7–10 g/kg in the final 24–36 h is optional; practised foods only).

**Taper:** volume — ≈70% below Build 2 volume (≈3 h of training before the race). Kept — Race-pace touches in every discipline. Removed — All long sessions and strength. Lifting — None — mobility and activation only. Monitor — Sleep (bank extra this week — race morning loses an hour to daylight saving), calm mind, appetite normal.

### Monday, Mar 8, 2027

**Objective:** Rest and recover — absorb the training.

#### Rest day
Rest · Key — mandatory · — · Rest

**Why:** Planned rest prevents the slow slide into fatigue that ruins key sessions. Sleep 7–9 h, eat normally (do not "save calories" on rest days), walk if you like. Race week begins. Print or save the Race-Day screen.

*Main set*
- No structured training. Optional: 20–30 min easy walk outdoors. — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* — —
*Fueling:* Carbohydrate ≈3–5 g/kg (250–415 g at 83 kg); protein ≈1.6–2.0 g/kg (135–165 g) spread over 4–5 meals. Hydrate normally; pale-yellow urine is a reasonable everyday check.
*Cues:* Bedtime 30 min earlier than usual; Fill in the daily check-in (sleep, fatigue, soreness)
*Success:* You actually rested
*Modifications:* illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Isaiah 26:3–4** (Peace) — God keeps in perfect peace those whose minds are steadfast because they trust in him; he is the Rock eternal.
>
> Race week. A steadfast mind is the best taper.
>
> _Choose peace over rumination this week._

### Tuesday, Mar 9, 2027

**Objective:** Maintain sharpness and confirm race power feels controlled while fatigue falls.

#### Race-week opener — 50 min
Opener · Key — mandatory · 50 min · Race power + short openers

**Why:** Tapering research supports cutting volume while keeping some intensity (Bosquet 2007; Mujika & Padilla 2003). Short race-pace efforts keep the engine tuned without adding fatigue.

*Warm-up*
- 15 min progressive — 62–72% FTP · HR 81–89% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 3–4/10
*Main set*
- 3 × 5 min at race power — 75–80% FTP · HR 86–92% bike LTHR · Cadence 82–92 rpm · RPE 5–6/10 · recovery: 3 min easy · _Note HR and RPE at race power; it should feel controlled._
- 3 × 30 s at 110% FTP — 108–112% FTP · Cadence 95–105 rpm · RPE 7–8/10 · recovery: 90 s easy
*Cool-down*
- 8 min easy spin, cadence 90+ — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Race bike on the road if safe (check shifting, brakes, tire pressure). Race bike exactly as it will be raced.
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Do not test fitness — fitness is banked; Check every piece of equipment
*Success:* Race power felt like RPE 5; Bike mechanically perfect
*Modifications:* fatigue: Ride 30 min easy with 2 × 3 min race power only. | weather: Trainer. | time: Minimum 30 min with the race-power reps. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Shake-out run — 15 min
Shake-out · Optional · 15 min · Z1

**Why:** Keeps rhythm during the taper. Off the bike — a mini brick.

*Main set*
- 12 min very easy — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
- 3 × 15 s strides / 60–90 s walk-jog — RPE 6–7/10 · _Fast and relaxed — about 5K effort, not a sprint. Tall posture, quick light feet._
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat Race shoes (confirm they feel right).
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required.
*Cues:* Feel light and quick
*Success:* Felt better at the end than at the start
*Modifications:* fatigue: Walk 15 min instead. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Joshua 3:5** (Preparation) — Joshua tells the people to consecrate themselves, because tomorrow the Lord will do amazing things.
>
> Consecrate the week: sleep, simple food, kind words, prayer. Preparation is spiritual too.
>
> _Set this week apart._

### Wednesday, Mar 10, 2027

**Objective:** Stay sharp and confident; rehearse starts and sighting.

#### Race-week sharpener — 1700
Taper swim · Key — mandatory · 35 min · 1700 yd/m · Race effort

**Why:** Short race-pace work keeps the feel for the water while fatigue falls.

*Warm-up*
- 300 easy + 4 × 50 drill — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Main set*
- 4 × 200 @ race effort, sight every 8 strokes — 20 s rest — CSS +3 to +6 s/100 · RPE 5–6/10
- 4 × 50: 15 strokes fast then settle — 30 s rest — RPE 5–8/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Calm, confident, smooth
*Success:* Felt fast and relaxed
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Psalm 89:9** (Trust) — You rule over the surging sea; when its waves mount up, you still them.
>
> A short sharpener swim. Whatever Lewisville Lake does on Sunday, God rules it.
>
> _Swim relaxed and confident._

### Thursday, Mar 11, 2027

**Objective:** Groove 70.3 run pace and rhythm.

#### Race-pace intervals 4 × 2 min — 40 min
Race-pace · Key — mandatory · 40 min · 70.3 race pace

**Why:** Rehearses goal pace on fresh-ish legs so it becomes automatic. Off the bike you will need to recognise it by feel and HR, not by forcing the watch.

*Warm-up*
- 10 min easy jog — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
- Drills: 2 × 20 m each — A-skips, high knees, butt kicks, carioca — RPE 3–4/10
- 4 × 20 s strides / 60–90 s walk-jog — RPE 6–7/10 · _Fast and relaxed — about 5K effort, not a sprint. Tall posture, quick light feet._
*Main set*
- 4 × 2 min at 70.3 race pace — 6% slower to 11% slower than threshold pace · HR 89–93% run LTHR · RPE 5–6/10 · recovery: 90 s easy · _Lock in the feel: breathing, cadence, effort._
*Cool-down*
- 10 min easy jog + 5 min walk — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat loop, track, or crushed-gravel trail; treadmill at 1% acceptable. Daily trainers, GPS watch, HR strap (wrist HR is unreliable during intervals).
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required. Optional: carbohydrate drink or mouth-rinse if training fasted. After: ~25–35 g protein + carbohydrate within ~1–2 h; if another key session is <24 h away, ~1–1.2 g/kg/h carbohydrate for the first few hours.
*Cues:* Practice taking a gel at race pace; Relaxed, efficient stride
*Success:* Pace within range at RPE ≤6
*Modifications:* fatigue: If fatigue ≥7/10, soreness ≥6/10 or sleep <6 h: run easy for 60–70% of the time, no intervals. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Romans 15:13** (Hope) — May the God of hope fill you with all joy and peace as you trust in him, so that you overflow with hope.
>
> Race pace feels easy this week — that is the taper working. Let joy and peace overflow.
>
> _Run today with joy._

### Friday, Mar 12, 2027

**Objective:** Active recovery and feel for the water.

#### Easy recovery swim — 1000
Recovery swim · Optional · 21 min · 1000 yd/m · Easy

**Why:** Low-stress swimming aids recovery and keeps the stroke fresh. Practice swim in Lewisville Lake if the official practice swim is offered; otherwise pool or rest. Travel/packet pickup.

*Warm-up*
- 300 easy — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Main set*
- 5 × 100 easy, alternating free / choice / pull — 15 s rest — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Cool-down*
- 200 easy choice, long and relaxed — CSS +10 to +18 s/100 · RPE 2–3/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Pool. Goggles (clear + tinted), cap, pull buoy, paddles (optional), fins (for drills), tempo trainer optional.
*Fueling:* Before: light carbohydrate snack if early (e.g., banana/toast). During: water bottle on deck. After: protein + carbohydrate meal within ~2 h.
*Cues:* Long, relaxed strokes
*Success:* Felt better leaving than arriving
*Modifications:* fatigue: Swap the main set for 6–8 × 100 easy with drills; swimming is a good "active recovery" day. | weather: Pool closed: replace with 20 min band/dryland pull work (straight-arm pulldowns, Y-T-W raises) + 20 min easy spin. | time: Keep warm-up 200 + main set; cut drill volume. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Numbers 6:24–26** (Blessing) — The Lord bless you and keep you; make his face shine on you and be gracious to you; turn his face toward you and give you peace.
>
> Travel and packet pickup: receive God's blessing over the weekend.
>
> _Pray this blessing over yourself and someone else._

### Saturday, Mar 13, 2027

**Objective:** Promote blood flow and recovery without adding meaningful training stress.

#### Recovery spin — 20 min
Recovery ride · Optional · 20 min · Z1 (active recovery)

**Why:** Light movement speeds the feeling of recovery and keeps the habit alive. Pre-race spin with 3 × 1 min at race power. Then bike check-in.

*Main set*
- 20 min continuous, flat route or trainer — 45–55% FTP · HR 68–80% bike LTHR · Cadence 85–95 rpm · RPE 1–2/10 · _If HR drifts above the target or legs feel heavy, shorten it — this ride should leave you fresher._
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat; trainer is ideal. Smart trainer or quiet road loop; power meter if available, HR strap, two bottles, fan indoors.
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required.
*Cues:* Light hands, relaxed shoulders; Cadence 90+ with almost no pressure on the pedals
*Success:* Finished feeling better than you started; Never exceeded RPE 3
*Modifications:* fatigue: Replace with a 20-min walk or full rest. | weather: Trainer. | time: Any duration ≥15 min still helps; skipping is acceptable. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

#### Shake-out run — 10 min
Shake-out · Optional · 10 min · Z1

**Why:** Keeps rhythm during the taper. Pre-race shake-out.

*Main set*
- 7 min very easy — 20% slower to 32% slower than threshold pace · HR 75–86% run LTHR · RPE 2–3/10
- 3 × 15 s strides / 60–90 s walk-jog — RPE 6–7/10 · _Fast and relaxed — about 5K effort, not a sprint. Tall posture, quick light feet._
*Terrain/equipment:* Flat Race shoes (confirm they feel right).
*Fueling:* Before: normal meal 2–3 h prior, or a 20–40 g carbohydrate snack 30–60 min prior if training early. During: water to thirst. No carbohydrate required.
*Cues:* Feel light and quick
*Success:* Felt better at the end than at the start
*Modifications:* fatigue: Walk 15 min instead. | weather: Heat >85 °F: run early, slow pace targets by 10–20 s/mi and use HR/RPE. Ice or lightning: treadmill at 1% grade. | time: Keep the warm-up, cut the main set by one-third. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **Zephaniah 3:17** (Identity) — The Lord your God is with you, the Mighty Warrior who saves; he takes great delight in you, quiets you with his love, and rejoices over you with singing.
>
> The night before the race. God already delights in you — before the swim, before the finish line, regardless of the clock.
>
> _Fall asleep resting in God's delight. (Clocks spring forward tonight.)_

### Sunday, Mar 14, 2027

**Objective:** Execute the plan: controlled swim, disciplined bike, strong final 5K.

#### RACE DAY — IRONMAN 70.3 Dallas–Little Elm
Race · Key — mandatory · 5 h 05 min · Race

**Why:** Everything since October 5 was built for today. Your fitness is set — execution decides the result. Open the Race-Day screen for splits, power/pace bands, fueling, and checklists.

*Warm-up*
- Wake 3–3.5 h before start (remember: clocks spring forward tonight). Breakfast 1–2 g/kg carbohydrate (~80–160 g), familiar foods. — RPE 1/10
- 10 min easy jog + arm swings, or 5–10 min easy swim if a warm-up area is available — RPE 2–3/10
*Main set*
- Swim 1.2 mi: seed yourself honestly in the rolling start; first 200 controlled; sight every 6–8 strokes; draft where legal and safe — CSS +3 to +6 s/100 · RPE 5–6/10
- Bike 56 mi: first 15 min below race power; then race power; climbs ≤ ~95% FTP briefly; eat & drink on schedule — 75–80% FTP · HR 86–92% bike LTHR · RPE 5–6/10
- Run 13.1 mi: mile 1–2 at 10–15 s/mi slower than goal; settle at goal pace/HR; decide at mile 10 whether to accelerate — 6% slower to 11% slower than threshold pace · HR 89–94% run LTHR · RPE 5–8/10
*Cool-down*
- Walk, eat, rehydrate. Thank God and the volunteers. — RPE 1/10
*Terrain/equipment:* Lewisville Lake / North Texas rolling roads / Little Elm trail (crushed gravel) See Race-Day checklist.
*Fueling:* Bike: 60–90 g/h carbohydrate (what you practised), fluid to thirst/sweat rate, sodium per your sweat test. Run: 40–60 g/h (gels + course drinks), water at every aid station, cola/caffeine late if practised.
*Cues:* Race your plan, not other athletes; Patience is a weapon
*Success:* Executed the pacing plan; Fueled on schedule; Ran the final 5K as strong or stronger than the first
*Modifications:* weather: Strong wind: pace by power/HR, not speed. Heat: slow run pace by HR, more cooling. Cold water: warm up on land, start calmly. | illness: Pain that changes your gait or stroke, chest pain, unusual breathlessness, dizziness, fainting, fever, or illness below the neck: stop, do not train through it, and seek medical evaluation.

> **2 Timothy 4:6–8** (Faithfulness) — Paul says he has fought the good fight, finished the race, and kept the faith; a crown of righteousness awaits him and all who long for Christ's appearing.
>
> Race day. Paul's crown was for faithfulness, not speed. Run your race with discipline and joy; your worth was settled long before the start line.
>
> _Whatever the clock says, finish today grateful._
